Lunenburg Public LibraryTrustees of Lunenburg Public Library, Meeting Minutes January 7, 2025Attending:  Harry Kubetz (remote), Dick Mailloux, Donna Saiia, Maia Wentrup (remote),Jana-Lee Balish (remote), Muir Haman – Director LPLThe LPLBT meeting was called to order at 7:16 p.m. by Acting Chairperson Dick Mailloux.Agenda Items●Joint meeting with Select Board RE:  Appointment of D. Noise - Member term to expire May 2025The Library Trustees met with David Niose at their last board meeting and voted in favor of asking him to become amember of the board. The Select Board asked questions of the candidate and then Dick made a motion to appoint DavidNiose to the Lunenburg Public Library Board of Trustees, seconded by Tom Alonzo. The two boards voted unanimously toappoint David Niose to the Lunenburg Public Library Board of Trustees.Adjourn●A motion to adjourn was made by Dick and seconded by Donna.  Meeting was adjourned.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   FY25 Budget Update 
 
   Staff Update 
I will meet with Deb Goodsell, the new Director for the COA, on Thursday afternoon. We plan to 
discuss coordinating and collaborating on programming and a potential joint grant opportunity. She 
and I have already discussed regular collaborative meetings.  
I trained Kaitee on book common book repairs, and she will help us moving forward to keep the 
volume of books trapped in “Damaged” status to a minimum. 
We have been working on coordinating the renovation project with staff to minimize disruption. 
Construction should start January 24th with the two new offices, then progress to the demolition and 
changes to the Teen Room space. At this point, we are estimating two months total for the work. 
Although there will not be dedicated room space for teens, the majority of the collection should still 
be accessible once it is setup in the Main Hall, and the rest will be in storage. Teen visitors will have a 
designated computer bank and area during afterschool hours, where Kaitee will be on duty to assist. 
We have asked the schools, and Boys and Girls Club to include informational details in their 
newsletters. We will send out a special newsletter message with details of the renovation plan, too. 
Announcements will also be made on social media, and requested to be made by the Select Board. 
Our Budget requests are due to the Town Manager by January 21, and I have prepared our list of 
requests. 
I will have School Resource Officer Munroe offer an informal staff training at our February staff 
meeting on de-escalating interactions and approaching patron interactions with respect. I am also

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
purchasing a staff training resource that is aimed at interacting with unhoused populations and has 
the same focus of approaching with respect and de-escalation. 
Our holiday donation drives were also successful: we hosted the Salvation Army Angel Tree that is 
usually at the Town Hall and a drive for the Food Pantry. Both benefited from the successful 
programs this month. More than 30 Angels gave gifts and the Food pantry bin needed to be emptied 
multiple times. What a generous community! 
I also met with the Director of Teaching and Learning for the Lunenburg Schools, Deborah Bookis 
January 3. She had inquired about research databases and was looking to see if there was any 
overlap in the resources we offer the community. The discussion stretched wider and Deborah 
seems like a good ally to support our goal of working with the schools to better serve our 
community. The First Annual Primary School Holiday Book Swap was a successful venture for us. We 
contributed over 100 books to the swap, had two Librarians attend the swap and generated some 
excitement in the kids who participated. The partnership did not translate into a huge number of 
library card signups, and only a handful of kids completed the accompanying scavenger hunt at the 
library. 
   Programs 
A number of large and very successful programs this time of year, combined with tradition and 
perhaps new traditions. Our Small Works @ LPL was fantastic. Fewer sales than I hoped for, but 
many visitors and we sold two of the higher priced works. 
The Artisan Showcase and Holiday Concert had the makings of a new tradition for the community. 
Dozens visited the vendors and more than 60 attended that concert. Money raised for the Friends, 
food and donations made to the Food Pantry and Salvation Army Angel Tree. We have some ideas 
for next year, and will continue to work with Kate and her vendors. 
Our craft program 12/5 drew more than 70 registrants, and our Polar Express night was nice crowd of 
27 kids. Our gingerbread house building and decorating programs were also popular, with a session 
for teens (18) and two for children and families (64). 
In the new year, Board Game Brunch, a new series, started Jan 11 at 10am. A half dozen attendees 
were very appreciative of the opportunity! The Music Clinics started again in December and two 
sessions each had 12 attendees. 24 puzzlers attended the puzzle race this month! And Craft and Chill 
continues to successfully bring in 13 or so, with many new faces. 
Coming up: Marc Berger returns for a Concert on January 18.. A number of teen and tween 
workshops this month: bullet journaling, job readiness, crafts, and TAB. Our Movie Nights return on 
January 31, when we will show Beetlejuice, Beetlejuice! 
   Outreach 
Moderate success with the Primary School Book Swap collaboration. Meeting with the Director of 
Teaching and Learning was a good connection for the potential partnership with schools.  
===PAGE 6===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
In May, there will be an All Vendor Fair organized by Lunenburg Business Association. I discussed 
with Joeci future possibilities, perhaps coordinating the fair with our Spring Book Sale.  
   Building & Maintenance 
ARPA furniture is ordered. We will need to fund about $13,000 more for these items. State Aid will be 
available including our FY25 award amount of approx. $34,000. The Friends Endowment is another 
potential funding source, if we were request it. Unofficially, Chris Ruth advised we likely will not use 
all the contingency funds. 
   Technology 
I was incorrect on two items with technology in recent reports. Four Microsoft Office licenses are 
(currently) unaccounted for, and Suzor IT installed versions of Office that do not match the invoices 
we received. Furthermore, due an overdue invoice for these licenses (and other overdue invoices) 
our order for security cameras has been delayed.

HIGHLIGHTS FOR DECEMBER: 
PATRON COMMENTS:  Found on my desk- 
===PAGE 8===
 
 
Family Craft Night:   
Great turn-out for this annual event!   80 kids and families stopped by the library after our Tree 
Lighting Event to make their “library” ornament.  We also offered hot chocolate to help with warm up 
after this cold outdoor event! 
 
Sewing 101: The kids gathered in the Story Craft Room to sew Christmas stockings for their stuffies- 
these kids are getting really good at sewing! 
Lego Challenge Day: 
We set up the tables in the Story Craft Room with 4 different Lego challenges during the school break 
week after Christmas 
Drop-In Craft: Over 40 people met in the Story/Craft Room for our December Drop-In Craft and 
made Santas!  This activity continues to be one of our most popular events- great job, Joslyn! 
Family Gingerbread House Building Event:  Wow- very enthusiastic participation in this annual 
event! 

===PAGE 9===
   
 
 Many variations on a theme        
Polar Express Event- We hosted a wonderful event this year featuring our new Santa’s Helper.  When our 
past helper was unable to make it, Chris Wiley stepped in and did a fabulous job reading this popular story.  He 
made it a memorable time for the kids and families in attendance.  And, as always, the kids were very excited, 
and creative, making candy trains! 

===PAGE 10===
 
Lego Challenge Day: 
We set up the tables in the Story Craft Room with 4 different Lego challenges during the school break week 
after Christmas.  Good turnout for this fun event. 
Lunch and a Movie: We hosted this fun event during the week after Christmas.  21 kids and families 
gathered in the Community Room to make mac and cheese, applesauce and brownies!  They all participated 
in mixing, measuring and preparing this feast, then they got to enjoy their lunch while watching the Paddington 
movie.

===PAGE 13===
Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
January 2025 
Summary of Last Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ We restarted the Saturday morning music clinics on December 7th with Lunenburg music teacher 
Jason Graham. And it was a great success for the first session in the series with 12 attendees. 
➢ The winter artisan showcase and December concert took place on Sunday December 8th throughout 
the library. We had over 30 vendors selling their crafts inside from 9 am to 1 pm, Santa took pictures 
from 9 am to noon, and Chris and Katie Laford entertained an audience of 62 people with their Celtic 
Christmas concert from 2 – 3:15 pm. It was a smashing success for the library and despite the 
amount of effort it took to coordinate, I think this is something we will definitely consider doing as a 
new annual tradition! 
➢ 24 puzzle enthusiasts joined us for a special holiday puzzle race on December 17th from 6 – 7 pm. It 
was a very challenging 500-piece puzzle, but the teams had a wonderful time working together to 
finish what they could in the allotted time. We will host another puzzle race during the February 
school vacation week. 
➢ Craft and Chill took place on a snowy evening on December 20th after the library closed at 6 pm. 
There were 13 people who braved the elements for cookie decorating, recipe sharing, and hot cocoa 
sipping. Of note were four women who each brought a teen son with them for a group mother-son 
outing. They all had a blast and appreciated that the library offers programs that support a multi-
generational experience. 
➢ I ran a totally new in-house created program for the first time: an all-ages holiday music bingo 
program the day before Christmas Eve. We had huge registration numbers, with over 40 people 
expressing interest in attending, so we prepared accordingly. However, only 23 people came for 
holiday music bingo that day, which ended up working out for the best as the rounds went by much 
quicker than anticipated. We gave out mini trophies to winners and were able to do a few extra 
rounds. Ages of attendees varied from toddlers to seniors, but all enjoyed the experience. We may 
attempt another music bingo program in the future.  
➢ On December 30th the library held the introductory “Sew Simple” meeting for anyone interested in 
learning to sew to meet, discuss potential projects, materials that may be needed, and other details 
for our monthly meetings going forward. Only three people were in attendance, but I am optimistic we 
will have better success at the Sew Simple sessions this year. 
 
This Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ The next Saturday morning music clinic with Jason Graham will be on January 4th. 
➢ We are hosting a new monthly Saturday program: board game brunch on January 11th for patrons to 
stop by for coffee and snacks while making new friends over the library’s stash of board games. 
➢ I will be running a DIY workshop for household cleaners on Wednesday January 15th at 6:00 pm. 
➢ The January Craft & Chill program will be held on Friday January 17th. Participants will make New 
Year wish bracelets with a design, color, or word to help them in their new year resolution.  
➢ The January music concert will be on Saturday January 18th with a return performance from Marc 
Berger. He will play a mix of folk and Americana music for patrons that Saturday afternoon.  
➢ Melanie will be leading a workshop to teach patrons about hoopla on the morning of Wednesday 
January 22nd.  This is the first in a series of digital services workshops run with Melanie’s help.  
===PAGE 14===
➢ On Tuesday January 28th we will be hosting our first workshop in the Sew Simple sessions for 
patrons to work on projects together, troubleshoot sewing machines, learn to read patterns, and 
support each other in the process.  
➢ We cap off the month with the return of Monthly Movie Nights on Friday January 31st. Joslyn has 
taken the lead with the program series and will be showing the recent release Beetlejuice Beetlejuice 
that evening after we close.  
Recurring Program Update 
➢ Book clubs are going well with attendance continuing to grow. 
o 100 Books Bucket List book club meets every first Tuesday morning. We averaged 9 
attendees at each meeting last year, and we continue to get new members each month.  
o The Brighter Days book club meets every second Wednesday evening. There are 8 
consistent members each month and we are building bonds of friendship after each book 
discussion. The books may miss the mark for “uplifting reads” but they generate deep and 
meaningful conversations among our readers.  
➢ The Community Chat & Color program was initially somewhat successful but has fallen off the last 
two months. We will pull this program from the offerings in February to free up space for other ideas. 
➢ Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group attendance has bloomed in recent months with some weekly 
meetings having nearly a dozen crafters! There are consistently at least 4-5 people here every 
Tuesday, but most weeks we will have 6-7 people with us for the drop-in program. Quite a hit!

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   FY25 Budget Update 
 
   FY26 Budget Update  
I discussed the library budget a number of times with ITM Carter Terenzini, and he made a final 
recommendation of $625,000 for the library budget. This recommendation included funding for 
100 floating hours to help us cover staff illnesses and still meet our Operation Safety Policy to 
remain open. It also includes a 10% increase to staff salaries as required by recent negotiations 
and updates to the Collective Bargaining Agreement and Salary Admin Plan. 
   Staff Update 
Deb Goodsell, Angela Clement, Michelle Belleza and I are planning to have monthly meetings to 
discuss collaborations and increase communication and coordination between departments.  
The renovation is well on its way toward completion. We have set up temporary shelving in the Main 
Hall, and stored a small number of books from the Teen Room. Work has been only minimally 
disruptive, with relatively little noise and mess. I anticipate moving to the new office within the 
week. The Story/Craft Room floor should be done when we return on Tuesday morning this week. 
We anticipate delivery of furniture on March 4. 
My presentation to the Finance Committee will be the night of our Trustee meeting. This will wrap up 
a very budget focused stretch of time. 
The Children’s Room, in particular, continues to be very busy, especially on Tuesday and Friday 
afternoons. The sheer volume of programming, though, also contributes to lots of activity. Our

preparations for the renovation have had only minimal impact on the volume of teens after school.

state-wide contest which pits communities head-to-head each week to see who proportionally can

attendance, too!

Teen Room itself. 
   Technology 
Two of the newest batch of Public Access Computers needed hard drive replacements and were 
shipped to the manufacturer for repair. They’ve been back for about two weeks, and we will keep a 
close eye on the other two for similar issues.

Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
February 2025 
Summary of Last Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ January started with another music clinic and performance workshop with local music teacher Jason 
Graham on Saturday, January 4th. We had 6 people attend. 
➢ Our launch of a new Saturday monthly series also started last month with the first Board Game 
Brunch happening on Saturday January 11th. There were 7 of us in attendance to play a variety of 
board games while sipping on coffee and eating snacks.  
➢ There were 20 people who came out on a Wednesday evening to make all natural DIY household 
cleaners using easily accessible materials. We made spray cleaners, fizzy toilet cleaner tablets, and 
a laundry scent booster. 
➢ Also in that week, we hosted the monthly Craft & Chill program for teens and adults. Participants 
made new year resolution bracelets with symbolic colors, patterns, beads and other supplies to 
remind them of a mantra or wish for 2025. Despite the weather concerns, we had 12 people attend. 
➢ Our Monthly Music Concert was on a Saturday afternoon in January. Marc Berger returned for a third 
time to the library and played his original Americana/folk music for 12 people in the community room. 
➢ Melanie led a workshop for patrons to learn more about Hoopla on a Wednesday morning. There 
were 4 people who came and left with some helpful information about this new digital resource. 
➢ Our first Sew Simple Session, a new open 2-hour time frame for patrons to come sew together, was 
at the end of January. There were 5 of us in attendance and the program was positively reviewed 
and appreciated by everyone there.  
 
This Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ The next Saturday morning music clinic with Jason Graham was on February 1st. 
➢ The next board game brunch was on February 8th. 
➢ We had a Winter Puzzle Swap on February 15th. 
➢ Another weight strengthening series with local occupational therapist Niki Holtzman started on 
February 18th. This will run on Tuesday mornings, 10-11 am, for five weeks.  
➢ Due to weather, we rescheduled a winter sowing workshop a few times and will hopefully hold it on 
February 20th at 5:00 pm  
➢ Partnering with the Lunenburg Fire Department, there will be a CPR & first aid training session at the 
library on Saturday February 22nd.  
➢ The next Sew Simple session will be held on February 25th. 
➢ An exciting program we are looking forward to this month, is the visible mending workshop featured 
on the front page of the Sentinel & Enterprise last week. There is a full class with a full waitlist and 
more interest keeps coming in. This will be held on Friday February 28th in the afternoon in our 
conference room. Due to the demand, we will inquire about booking another session with her. 
➢ There will be a movie night on the same night, Friday, February 28th, with a screening of Wicked that 
evening.  
➢ Sadly, there is no monthly music concert this month due to a series of miscommunications.  
===PAGE 11===
Recurring Program Update 
➢ Book clubs are going well with attendance continuing to grow. 
o The Baking Book Club, which meets every first Wednesday, has not generated much 
growth in attendance, but there are 3 of us who faithfully attend each month and are 
building strong bonds together. One of our members is determined to get new attendees 
so she talks it up at other town events. We had one new face this month so perhaps it’s 
working! We are also discussing options to generate more interest, such as moving the 
date and time or changing to a general cookbook club with other dishes welcome. 
o The 100 Books Bucket List book club meets every first Tuesday morning. We averaged 9 
attendees at each meeting last year, and we continue to get new members each month.  
o The Brighter Days book club meets every second Wednesday evening. There are 8 
consistent members each month and we are building bonds of friendship after each book 
discussion. This month we had 2 new members join us and are discussing new ideas for 
next year’s theme. We may draw titles from a pool of reader generated recommendations. 
The current books on our list may miss the mark for “uplifting reads” but they generate 
deep and meaningful conversations among our readers.  
➢ Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group attendance has bloomed in recent months with some weekly 
meetings having nearly a dozen crafters! There are consistently at least 4-5 people here every 
Tuesday, but most weeks we will have 6-7 people with us for the drop-in program. Quite a hit!

Staff can have this sheet available for those patrons who are receptive toreceiving help.○FY2026 Budget UpdateThe FY26 budget has increases in the water and sewer accounts to adjust for the increased cost of boththese expense categories. There is no indicationto the users that an item is AI generated. When a patron checks out an item, the library is charged. This articlebrought a lot of nationwide attention to this issue which led to Hoopla saying they had removed most of the AIitems in their database.  Muir said that libraries can not curate these for profit databases. You need to offer it all toyour patrons.○Muir made us aware of a lawsuit in California against Hoopla for tracking users.○Muir also made us aware that AI companies are requesting public records from municipalities to use that data totrain AI.○Harry asked if we should cancel Hoopla.  Muir is going to do a cost/benefit analysis and get back to us.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
It seems like the last few weeks have been as busy as any for me. User’s Council, Security Camera 
installs, the finish of the Teen Room renovation, patrons visiting to chat about changes at the library. 
I participated in training sessions: Census Data, Public Records Law, and Chief Thibedeau of the 
Police Department visited our staff meeting this week to give us an informal training on de-
escalation techniques. He also talked to us about opioid overdose warning signs, and building 
security procedures.  
I also met with the EV charging design firm to discuss their design and why it deviated from what we 
had hoped for. I’ve reached out to Deb Bookis of the schools to explore partnerships and I provided 
the schools feedback on their draft Collection Development Policy. 
The new Town Manager hosted a townwide staff luncheon March 17 and is definitely working on 
creating a healthier, friendlier atmosphere between departments. 8 library staff attended and we 
closed the library for 90 minutes. 
Bill Bernard of the DPW got a design for a rainwater garden to me last week. I shared it with Robin 
for her feedback. Bill and I discussed Tuesday morning, and he is supportive of the ideas we 
suggested. He will work with the design firm to incorporate what they can. I spoke with Susan 
Conroy briefly about the ideas for the memorial garden and gave her copies of our ideas. She 
seemed very surprised and appreciative, supportive of the ideas, but I want to connect again and 
confirm. Robin and I have chatted briefly about a tree stewarding program for the library grounds. 
We hope to germinate, plant and then steward a native tree for each of the invasive, decorative 
pears currently planted on the grounds. 
We are in the final steps of finding a replacement for our oldest Library Page, who has stepped away 
to concentrate on this studies and preparing for three AP tests this Spring. Interviews occurred 
yesterday.  
Monty tech students have reached out to offer volunteer labor. I will meet with them to discuss 
possibilities. Similarly, Lydia and I will meet with two community organizations—a career center and 
a support group for immigrants—to see if there are ways to partner in support of the surrounding 
communities. 
Lydia and I have developed our Staffing Plan and are finalizing individual projects for each staff 
member. These will be assigned in the next two weeks. Lydia, Melanie, and I are working on 
finalizing an Outreach Plan, identifying groups by their obstacle to library use. I will present this next 
month, and share it as soon as it is done. 
   Programs 
Programming attendance seems to be taking a “vacation” the last two weeks. The weather is maybe 
too good, and we’ve seen a drop in numbers. 
Only a handful attended our St. Pat’s concert. Teen programs have seen numbers drop. Even story 
time attendance is down a bit. Recent highlights include the partnership with Lunenburg Business 
Association for a program on downsizing, Joslyn’s drop in craft session with almost 40 attendees, 
===PAGE 6===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
Kaitee’s TAB group, Music Clinic (19), Jill Pottle’s Drawing Class 0funded by the LCC (8 students), and 
Board Game Brunch (still only 6 attendees, but they LOVE it!). 
Coming up: Appraisal Workshop 3/22, Retirement Planning with Nikki of Edward Jones 3/24, Movie 
Night: Conclave 3/28, tomorrow night’s Teen Web Development Workshop, Gardening with Roin for 
Kids 3/22. 
   Outreach 
Libby Workshop at LAAC on 3/19: we helped 4 individuals interested in ebooks and e-audiobooks. 
We are also working with the Friends to coordinate their attendance at Farmers and Artisan Markets, 
and events like the All Vendor Fair 5/10. 
Melanie, Lydia and I are finalizing our plan for next steps. Identifying target groups, services and how 
that will happen.  
   Building & Maintenance 
Our new Teen Room looks spectacular. The teens seem very excited and have been enjoying the new 
furniture.  
   Technology 
Half of the security cameras are installed. The other half are scheduled for tomorrow. We were also 
able to run 9 more cables to staff desks to get workstations off of wifi and back on LAN.

===PAGE 8===
 
 
HIGHLIGHTS FOR FEBRUARY: 
SENSORY STORY TIME WITH MISS PAM: 
Pam Wockenfuss presented Chicka Chicka Boom Boom sensory story time for an eager group of 11 children.   
She shared this wonderful story with the kids and their families and then allowed time to experience the story 
with all of their senses through the use of individual sensory boxes with a letter theme.   Parents/caregivers 
are encouraged to participate along with the children as Pam explains the many benefits of sensory play. The 
added benefit- which the kids love- is that they get to keep their individual sensory bins! 
Battlebots with Mobile Quest:   
In this epic workshop participants used their coding skills to design a robot and take it tothe MobileQuest Arena 
where they then battled for robot supremacy. The participants worked in small teams to modify a Dash robot with 
balloons, skewers, plastic straws, and more as they prepared for battle to see who would have the last Bot 
standing?! 
      
Preparing for battle     And they’re off!             Making modifications for 2nd round 
 
Cookie Decorating with Miss Madeline: 
 Miss Madeline stopped by to do another great cookie painting class with the kids. The kids had a blast bringing 
their delicious designs to life, exploring their artistic talents, and then enjoying a sweet treat they decorated 
themselves. It's a fun-filled, engaging activity that's topped off with a snack—what's not to like!   
     
 
 
Homeschoolers Craft Corner: 

===PAGE 9===
This popular monthly activity was well attended again as the kids got a chance to interact with each other, be 
creative and go home with a cool candle holder!  The homeschooling families are very appreciative of the time, 
space and activities that we provide for their group.   
 
 So lovely!

Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
March 2025 
Summary of Last Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ February’s music clinic and performance workshop with Jason Graham had 18 people attend. 
➢ Board Game Brunch had 6 players on Saturday February 8th.  
➢ Our Winter Puzzle Swap on Saturday February 15th saw 72 people come in to get new to them 
puzzles from the 650+ selections on display. Our most popular puzzle swap of the year! 
➢ The winter session of Niki Holtzman’s weight strengthening series started on February 18th and will 
run for 5 weeks on Tuesday mornings. We have averaged 12 people at sessions, as of March 11th. 
➢ We hosted a puzzle race for 8 teams competing to complete their puzzle first on the evening of 
February 19th. There were 34 people of all ages, even a team of 3 young boys, participating in the 
event, with a waitlist of 3 additional teams who wanted to participate.  
➢ The rescheduled Winter Sowing Program was held on the evening of February 20th. As always, there 
was a strong showing of people coming to make a winter sowing project with 28 people at this year’s 
program. Robin Venezia proved indispensable in her assistance making this a success again, and 
working with her is always a pleasure! 
➢ This month’s Sew Simple session had 5 people on February 25th, but those who came made 
significant progress on their projects and appreciated the help and opportunity to work as a group. 
➢ The visible mending workshop we were looking forward to hosting was well attended with positive 
feedback from participants. We have a second one booked on April 18th to accommodate those who 
were waitlisted or missed the event in February. 
This Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ The March Saturday morning music clinic with Jason Graham was on March 1st with 19 people. 
➢ Following the music clinic on March 1st was a reception for the new art exhibit with Trish Brown. We 
had two people join us for that event. 
➢ Creative Drawing Basics was taught by Jill Pottle on Sunday March 2nd. There were 8 students in her 
session. This was funded by an LCC grant.  
➢ The Lunenburg Business Association partnered with the library to host a downsizing seminar in the 
Wallace Room on Thursday, March 13th. There were 12 people in attendance. 
➢ The March session of craft & chill, booked for Friday, March 14th, was canceled again due to low 
registrations. We may pause these programs until next fall/winter, when it was more popular.  
➢ Board Game Brunch took place on Saturday March 15th with 5 players at the library.  
➢ We were very excited to bring back The Gloucester Hornpipe and Clog Society for a St. Patrick’s Day 
concert on Sunday, March 16th. Unfortunately, a mix of weather and timing most likely led to an 
unexpectedly low turnout for the amazing concert. There were 5 people in attendance. 
➢ Our community read book discussion brunch with the Lunenburg Adult Activity Center had 16 
participants on the morning of March 17th. 
➢ An appraisal workshop will be held on Saturday, March 22nd.  
➢ Edward Jones Financial will visit the library on March 24th to discuss retirement planning.  
➢ The next Sew Simple session will be held on March 25th. 
➢ There will be a movie night on Friday, March 28th, with a screening of Conclave that evening.  
➢  Jill Pottle will teach a second LCC grant funded session of Creative Drawing Basics on Sunday, 
March 31st. 
===PAGE 12===
Recurring Program Update 
➢ Book clubs are going well with attendance continuing to grow in all but the Baking Book Club. 
o The Baking Book Club, which meets every first Wednesday, will have its last meeting in 
April. Due to continued low engagement we will be discontinuing this experimental book 
club. We may do quarterly recipe swap socials or another type of food-based program, but 
that is to be determined. 
o The 100 Books Bucket List book club meets every first Tuesday morning. We averaged 9 
attendees at each meeting last year, and we continue to get new members each month.  
o The Brighter Days book club meets every second Wednesday evening. There are now 10 
consistent members of that discussion group.   
➢ Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group attendance has bloomed in recent months with some weekly 
meetings having nearly a dozen crafters! There are consistently at least 4-5 people here every 
Tuesday, but most weeks we will have 6-7 people with us for the drop-in program. Quite a hit! 
Virtual Program Update 
➢ These are all events sponsored and managed by other libraries. By partnering with them we receive 
a dedicated link to track viewership and engagement from our patrons. To further promote these 
events, there is a standalone brochure at the front desk advertising March and April virtual programs. 
o “Author Afarin Bellisario discusses "Silenced Whispers"” took place on March 8th. Viewers TBD. 
o “Freedom to Read Event: Zoom Interview with Amanda Jones, author of That Librarian” took place 
on March 16th. We had 0 viewers. 
o “Irish Whiskey - History and Recs with Travis Ireland of The Friskey Whiskey Podcast” will be on 
March 17th. 
o “Community Read: Author Talk with Sonia Purnell!” will be on March 25th. 
o “A History of Guinness Beer with Brewery Ambassador, Mike Reardon” will be on March 31st.

House.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   FY25 Budget Update 
 
   Staff Update 
The library shared calls to participate in “Empowered by Libraries” from MBLC, an advocacy 
opportunity to tell stories about how libraries have been important in your lives. We have bookmarks 
and postcards that people can write a short narrative about what libraries mean and submit to 
lovemasslibraries.com. I also requested the Select Board sign on to a letter initiated by Mayor Petty 
of Worcester asking our Congressional Delegation to voice their resistance to IMLS cuts. Chair 
Person Nuzzolo signed on behalf of the SB after their April 1 meeting.  
Melanie, Lydia and I have worked extensively the last few weeks to finalize a proposal for Outreach 
Services. Our plan is attached. 
I have discussed and assigned all staff projects in the staffing plan. Joslyn and Lydia are particularly 
about their projects.  
Kaitee is on FMLA, no return date yet. I met with Deb Bookis, Director of Learning and Teaching at 
the Schools to discuss partnerships and the schools draft of a Reconsideration Policy. There are 
several promising possibilities of partnerships, notably around STEM, OpSciEd curriculum and school 
library support. I will meet with Deb and Tara (STEM Lab) next. 
   Programs 
Kate Chadbourne’s concert had excellent turn out and was an impressive performance as always. 
Kate is also interested in volunteering a workshop for students who are preparing the college essays. 
Jason Graham’s music clinic continues to draw a nice crown, which was up to 19 in March and 20 in 
April.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
Craft and Chill last Friday night had great turn out, too, with 27 signed up and 23 attendees. Monday’s 
grief support group was well attended and received positive feedback from the facilitator. She loved 
our library. This group was formerly meeting at LAAC. Tuesday night we hosted an Intro to 
Homeschooling program at the request of a local resident. 
Virtual Programming has offered a variety of topics, with low engagement. Most registrations seem 
to be people hoping to receive the recording to watch at a later date.  
Children’s Programming this month has been spectacular. Sensory Story Time with Pam Wockenfuss, 
Art with Julie, and STEM programming with Chris Rodrigues twice. Excellent experiences for 
attendees. 
Coming up: Thoreau’s “Economy” Reading 4/16, Visible Mending WKSP #2 4/18, Animal Adventures 
4/22, Japanese Folktales and Origami 4/23,  Crafternoon: Poem in Your Pocket 4/24, Open Mic Poetry 
Night 4/30 
   Outreach 
Outreach Plan proposal has been the focus this month. 
   Building & Maintenance 
The new cleaning crew continues to have trouble entering the building without setting off the alarm. 
They are tasked with cleaning everything, so we are keeping an eye on items that might get left out 
to correct now. 
The Proctor Memorial Garden water feature should return this coming week. Test pits for the 
rainwater garden happened last week. 
Susan Conroy and family are supportive of our plans and are very interested in helping with their 
labor to create the garden. 
   Technology 
Another of the new batch of Dell public access computers broke this week. I am reaching out to the 
vendor to see what can be done. This instance pushes the likelihood of a manufacturing defect up. 
Mason should be completing an install of security monitor in the Director’s Office this week. Melanie 
and I are planning the process of our ongoing digital resource assessment.

===PAGE 7===
 
PATRON QUOTE FOR MARCH: 
“I love your library!  I live in Leominster and work in Brookline, New Hampshire and this is the 
library that I choose to come to. I love coming here to your library!” 
 
HIGHLIGHTS FOR MARCH: 
SENSORY STORY TIME WITH MISS PAM: 
Pam Wockenfuss presented Light Up the Night sensory story time for an eager group of 10 children.   She 
shared this wonderful story with the kids and their families and then allowed time to experience the story with 
all of their senses through the use of individual sensory boxes.   Parents/caregivers are encouraged to 
participate along with the children as Pam explains the many benefits of sensory play. Lots of thought and 
sensory development awareness goes into developing the bins and I love that the kids get to keep them.
  
ART CLASS WITH MISS JULIE 
We held our first art class with Julie Harrold and the children loved it! Julie has a BFA in Studio Arts, is a former 
Elementary art teacher/Art director and currently a homeschool mom who also teaches art classes in her spare 
time.  She has a wonderful rapport with the kids, and they all benefit from her supportive, encouraging manner of 
teaching. 

===PAGE 8===
                
 
 
S T E M WITH CHRIS:  
Two great S T E M classes with Chris this month-  
Simple Circuits: This workshop focused on creative thinking, and problem solving through circuit wiring concepts 
such as parallel and series circuits.  Experimentation was key as the kids built their projects and got to see what 
didn’t work and they worked to find a solution that would. 
Hydraulic Critters: In this class, the kids learned about how hydraulics work while creating a hydraulic bug.   
                    
MOTHER’S DAY SEED PLANTING: 
On a cold, drizzly Saturday day we gathered in the Story Craft Room for a two-part plant event.  At first, we talked 
about gardening – how the process of gardening starts and what plants need to grow.  Then we planted some 
marigold seeds.  
As the seeds sprout and begin to develop, library visitors can watch the seedlings grow until they are ready to 
become a gift for someone special. The librarians here at LPL will take good care of the growing plants until the kids 
come back in May to transplant the baby plants into bigger pots and decorate them for Mother’s Day gifting.

Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
April 2025 
Summary of Last Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ The March Saturday morning music clinic with Jason Graham was on March 1st with 19 people. 
➢ Following the music clinic on March 1st was a reception for the new art exhibit with Trish Brown. We 
had two people join us for that event. 
➢ Creative Drawing Basics was taught by Jill Pottle on Sunday March 2nd. There were 8 students in her 
session. This was funded by an LCC grant.  
➢ The Lunenburg Business Association partnered with the library to host a downsizing seminar in the 
Wallace Room on Thursday, March 13th. There were 12 people in attendance. 
➢ The March session of craft & chill on Friday, March 14th was canceled again due to low registrations. 
➢ We were very excited to bring back The Gloucester Hornpipe and Clog Society for a St. Patrick’s Day 
concert on Sunday, March 16th. Unfortunately, a mix of weather and timing most likely led to an 
unexpectedly low turnout for the amazing concert. There were 5 people in attendance. 
➢ Our community read book discussion brunch with the Lunenburg Adult Activity Center had 16 
participants on the morning of March 17th. 
➢ An appraisal workshop was held on Saturday, March 22nd. We had 13 people visit the library to get 
their items appraised. There was some initial confusion on timing of the program, but I think those 
who attended enjoyed the event.  
➢ Edward Jones Financial visited the library on March 24th to discuss retirement planning, however we 
did not have anyone come to the program. We may try another topic at another time. 
➢ Jill Pottle came back to teach a second LCC grant funded session of Creative Drawing Basics on 
Sunday, March 31st to a full class of 10 students.  
This Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ On Saturday April 5th we had 20 people join us for another session of the music clinic series. 
➢ Our rescheduled Empathy in Grief workshop was canceled again due to low registrations. We may 
try again in the fall or create a handout of information to have at the library for patrons to take home.  
➢ Janet Winer, Abigal Perrine, and I have launched community information sessions for the public to 
learn about the upcoming 20th anniversary of the library building. We held one on April 8th that was 
not well attended but have two more booked for the end of the month. 
➢ Our craft & chill program for April was an LCC funded paint night on April 11th. We had 20 people join 
us for a fun family night out that was a huge hit! 
➢ Monthly Music Series: Irish Poetry & Song with Kate Chadbourne was on Sunday April 13th and drew 
35 attendees for another stellar performance by Kate! 
➢ The library is also the new meeting location for the local grief and healing support group that formerly 
met at the Lunenburg Adult Activity Center. Their first session will be on Monday April 14th.  
➢ A homeschooling advocacy group will present a basic information session on Tuesday April 15th. 
➢ On Wednesday April 16th we will host Jay DiPrima for a return program with Henry David Thoreau. 
This time he will be reading Thoreau’s work “Economy” in period costume. 
➢ We have a return visit from Cathy Karp for another Visible Mending Workshop on Friday April 18th.  
➢ During the school vacation week there will be another Puzzle Race on Wednesday April 23rd.  
➢ To cap off poetry month, we will be hosting an Open Mic Poetry Night on Wednesday April 30th for 
anyone to come and share their favorite poem with the community.  
===PAGE 11===
Recurring Program Update 
➢ Book clubs are doing well with excellent discussions at our monthly meetings. 
o The Baking Book Club ended after our April meeting had no attendees. We may revisit in 
the fall, but for now we will take a break from this to consider other options.  
o The 100 Books Bucket List book club meets every first Tuesday morning. We averaged 9 
attendees at each meeting last year, and we continue to get new members each month.  
o The Brighter Days book club meets every second Wednesday evening. There are now 10 
consistent members of that discussion group.  
➢ Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group attendance remains steady at 5-6 per week. And new members 
join periodically so it continues to grow! 
➢ The monthly Board Game Brunch programs happening on a Saturday morning have been 
successful. We have a manageable number of 5-8 people at each event.  
➢ Monthly Movie Nights are gaining momentum as word spreads about them. The March program had 
13 people in the audience, and they gave very positive feedback about the film. 
➢ Sew Simple Sessions have petered out with very limited engagement from patrons. We have had no 
more than two people at each session since it started in December. Due to the low attendance and 
drain on staff resources, April will be the last program in the series so we can invest in other areas.  
Virtual Program Update 
➢ These are all events sponsored and managed by other libraries. By partnering with them we receive 
a dedicated link to track viewership and engagement from our patrons. To further promote these 
events, there is a standalone brochure at the front desk advertising March and April virtual programs. 
We have not joined program offerings for May or beyond at this time. 
 
o “Irish Whiskey - History and Recs with Travis Ireland of The Friskey Whiskey Podcast” was on 
March 17th. We had 2 register but no one joined the program.  
o “Community Read: Author Talk with Sonia Purnell!” was on March 25th. We did not track stats for 
engagement from specific community groups, but there were 25 total attendees for the talk. 
o “A History of Guinness Beer with Brewery Ambassador, Mike Reardon” was on March 31st. There 
were some technical issues with the connection, but we had 4 people register and 1 joined the talk. 
o “Local Farms, MA - Produce, Meats, Dairy, Oh My!” was on April 7th. We had 4 people register and 
2 attend the event live. 
o “The Intersection of Climate Action and Romance Novels: A Panel Discussion” was on April 10th. 
We had 1 register but did not attend the program live. 
o “Tudor Queens and Ladies in Waiting: A Discussion with Authors Nicola Clark and Jade Scott” was 
on April 12th. We had 2 register, and no one attended live. 
o “The Two Nerdy History Girls Ride Again!” will be on April 14th. 
o “No More Curse! A History of the Red Sox with David Kruh” will be on April 28th.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
Annual Town Meeting on May 3 was the big event in the last three weeks since we met. It was a long 
meeting, running until about %:30, but a positive one for the library (See preceeding Budget update), 
and approval of a capital plan that include HVAC design and planning for the library. The two large 
debt exclusions failed to achieve 2/3 majorities on the Town Meeting floor, but are on the ballot this 
Saturday, while the Citizen’s Petition Override of Prop 2 ½ to raise funds to give the schools $2.1M 
passed and is not on the ballot. 
The Friends approved money for the Conroy Memorial Garden, and I met with the artist I would like 
to work with to discuss the project. Kevin Boussom, son of Laura, is excited to work on the project 
and anticipates completing his work before the fall. Robin was able to source the remaining plants 
she needs from Central Mass Gardens, and we are likely to have a bed prep and planting day in the 
next few weeks (likely May 24). 
I met with Deb Bookis on 4/16 and hoping to meet with her and Tara (STEM club leader) to continue 
that conversation. Kaitee has returned from FMLA and is working on developing a partnership with 
Pam Vallee.  
Our SRP is finalized (save one day where we will have a program but have had one presenter unable 
to come through, leaving us scrambling). We will be serving meals again, M-Th at 12 noon from June 
23 to July 31. We will be working the same vendor as last year. 
We have started gathering data for our Staffing Workflow Study. This week all staff will log their 
tasks throughout the day and Lydia and I will compile and analyze afterward. Likely we will repeat 
the log during the SRP to deepen our data. Lydia and I have identified a few areas already that 
require some attention in our staff hierarchy to help clarify roles and assignments. The goal for this 
study is to document data that will support our requests for funding additional staff positions or 
adjustments to our current organizational hierarchy. All staff are working on their individual projects 
according to our staffing plan. I have put copies of the staff evaluation in our meeting folder for your 
review. I plan to complete individual evaluations and reviews during the summer for all staff. I am 
considering creating an rolling schedule for evaluations that would happen around hiring 
anniversaries to spread out the rather large task of reviews. 
Next week is the annual MLA conference on the Cape. Melanie and Joslyn are slated to go. I will 
attend a Mental Health First Aid training on May 20. I am also planning to get together with Library 
Directors from Leominster and Fitchburg to share ideas and progress. Ongoing Zoom calls between 
Directors on the topic of our current funding situations continue. 
   Programs 
Although our Open Mic Poetry Night did not bring crowds, Kate Chadbourne’s concert had excellent 
turn out and was an impressive performance as always. Kate is also interested in volunteering a 
workshop for students who are preparing the college essays. Jason Graham’s music clinic continues 
to draw a nice crown, which was up to 19 in March and 20 in April. 
Craft and Chill last in April had great turn out, too, with 27 signed up and 23 attendees. Monday’s 
grief support group was well attended and received positive feedback from the facilitator. She loved 
===PAGE 6===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
our library. This group was formerly meeting at LAAC. Tuesday night we hosted an Intro to 
Homeschooling program at the request of a local resident. 
Virtual Programming has offered a variety of topics, with low engagement. Most registrations seem 
to be people hoping to receive the recording to watch at a later date.  
Children’s Programming this month has been spectacular, especially for school vacation week. Art 
with Julie, and STEM programming with Chris Rodrigues, Japanese Folktales and Origami, Animal 
Adventures, the ongoing Tween cooking class. Excellent experiences for attendees. 
Coming up: Puzzle swap (5/17), Floral Design Workshop (5/13), Woman in Jazz (Virtual, 5/21), Creeque 
Alley returns for Monthly Music Series (5/29), and Captain America: Brave New World (5/30). 
   Outreach 
▪ Outreach 
We have been reaching out to potential partners for outreach services, including Arrive Lunenburg 
and Tri-Town Apartments, and Smokestack Coffee Roasters. We are assembling a Welcome to 
Lunenburg and the Library packet of resources. 
• All Vendor Fair at the T.C. Passios building - Saturday, May 10th, 11:00-6:00 
o Weather started rainy, and ended windy, but this was a positive event with solid 
attendance. Robin manned the booth with Melody from the Friends and were joined 
by Melanie and Joslyn from the staff. Chatted with about 15 people, about half were 
not regular library users.  
• Lunenburg Cruise Night at T.C Passios Wednesday May 21

▪ Building & Maintenance 
===PAGE 7===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
Our HVAC system needed attention early in May. THE board that had been repaired two years ago, 
needed some tweaks to programming and an electrical repair. The system seems to be operating 
efficiently again.  
The new cleaning crew seems to have learned how to enter the building without setting off the 
alarm. They are tasked with cleaning everything. So, we expect them to do so. Most departments in 
town reported having repeated issues at our last department head meeting. 
Still waiting for Lakeview to have time in their schedule to get the Proctor Memorial Garden water 
feature running again. Test pits for the rainwater garden happened last month, and Robin and I met 
with the design firm and Bill Bernard to discuss specifics of the latest draft design. The test pit results 
dictate that the garden function more as filter than as an aid to infiltration, and we have tempered 
the maintenance needs of the plantings with a focal point, so that our volunteer labor can effectively 
help establish the native plants that will be going in. The landscaping crew will be able to mow 
around the filter beds without disturbing the native plants. 
▪ Technology 
Another of the new batch of Dell public access computers broke mid-April. It has been sent to the 
manufacturer for repairs. 
Mason helped to install a security monitor in the Director’s Office mid-April. Melanie and I have 
begun to gather data for our ongoing digital resource assessment.

Kaitlyn Himmelberger 
Young Adult Services 
May 2025 
Summary 
- Programs 
o Past programs:  
▪ Needle Felting for Teens 
▪ Art on the Wing – 3 participants 
▪ TAB meeting – cancelled due to illness 
▪ Video Game Night – cancelled due to no participation 
▪ Virtual Cooking Class – 7 participants 
o Upcoming teen programs:  
▪ TAB Meeting 
▪ Homeschool Craft Corner 
▪ Drippy Sky Painting 
▪ End of School Henna 
Other news/updates 
- Art on the Wing was a very successful program. One of the teens in the program is 
interested in animal rehabilitation like the program presenters and got the 
opportunity to discuss animal rehab with them as a career field.  
- Video Game Nights: We only have one video game night left for the school year. I am 
thinking that next year we will only have video game nights quarterly instead of 
monthly since this year’s attendance has been so low.  
- Virtual Cooking classes continue to be the most popular teen program. It's also a 
good family program as younger siblings and parents get involved as well. 
- TAB: Currently we have 4 regular TAB members. I would like to see a couple more 
and I am hoping that as some of the active library tweens age up this summer, they 
will join TAB. Since June of last year TAB has collectively volunteered 47hours 15min 
in the library. They are planning to volunteer in the children’s room this summer to 
assist Debbie and Joslyn with summer reading.  
- The Hot Pepper Garden has moved from the Teen Room to the garden outside. I 
created lots of signage to hopefully get people interested as they walk by. We had a 
patron give us a ton more pepper plants so now we have 12 varieties of peppers in 
the garden.  
===PAGE 13===
- The SHAPIRO group teens will be joining us again this summer with daily visits. I 
have a meeting scheduled with the director to work out how these visits will look.  
- Teen Summer Reading: 
o Doing a mix of crafting, take & makes, and D&D this summer. Last summer 
attendance was very low despite the high number of teen readers, so I didn’t 
want to book anybody beyond the Henna program.  
o I have high hopes for the Take & Makes series as they were popular over the 
winter. The gardening take & make also did really well.  
o Continuing the strategy of high value prizes for summer reading that I think 
won over a lot of teens last year. This year’s big ticket items include a record 
player and polaroid camera.  
o I am in touch with the High School librarian, Pam Valle, to see how we can 
promote summer reading before school lets out. She passed on all the info 
for the program to the 8th grade ELA teachers.  
===PAGE 14===
Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
May 2025 
Summary of Last Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ On Saturday April 5th we had 20 people join us for another session of the music clinic series. 
➢ Janet Winer, Abigal Perrine, and I have launched community information sessions about the 
upcoming 20th anniversary of the library building. We held one on April 8th that was not well attended 
but the morning session on April 28th had two volunteers attend. 
➢ Our craft & chill program for April was an LCC funded paint night on April 11th. We had 20 people join 
us for a fun family night out that was a huge hit! 
➢ Monthly Music Series: Irish Poetry & Song with Kate Chadbourne was on Sunday April 13th and drew 
35 attendees for another stellar performance by Kate! 
➢ A homeschooling advocacy group gave an introductory information session on Tuesday April 15th to 
14 people with varying interests in homeschooling.  
➢ On Wednesday April 16th we hosted Jay DiPrima for a return program with Henry David Thoreau. 
This time he read Thoreau’s work “Economy” in period costume to 16 people. 
➢ We had a return visit from Cathy Karp for another Visible Mending Workshop on Friday April 18th. 
There were 8 people at this workshop. 
➢ During the school vacation week, we had five teams with a total of 18 participants join us for another 
Puzzle Race on Wednesday April 23rd.  
➢ To cap off poetry month, we attempted to host an Open Mic Poetry Night on Wednesday April 30th for 
anyone to come and share their favorite poem with the community. However, only one brave local 
poet came to recite his favorite work. Instead of holding the program, he and I had an in-depth 
conversation on poetry for a few minutes during which he shared some insights to help us next time. 
  
This Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ On Saturday May 1st we had 12 people join us for the final session of the music clinic series. Due to 
their success, we will negotiate continuing the series in the fall of this year.  
➢  There was a Floral Design Workshop on Tuesday May 13th. 
➢ The annual Spring Puzzle Swap will take place on Saturday May 17th. 
➢ Wednesday May 21st we will host the first of our Monthly Music Series programs with jazz flutist 
Galen Abdur-Razzaq joining us on Zoom for a tribute to women in jazz performance. 
➢ On Friday May 23rd, we will host an opening reception for the new art exhibit: Lunenburg High School 
AP & Honors Art Thesis Show. There will be a live jazz performance by two students at the reception 
that Friday from 4-6 pm. The exhibit will run May 17th-31st 
➢ The library will be hosting author Varla Ventura for a unique talk on botanical folklore based on 
information from her book Enchanted Plants on Wednesday May 28th.  
➢ May will have two music concerts as part of the Monthly Music Series, with the second event booked 
for Thursday May 29th when the folk-rock duo Creeque Alley return to perform. One of our most 
popular, well received, and enthusiastic performers, we are very excited to welcome them back! 
Recurring Program Update 
➢ Book clubs are doing well with excellent discussions at our monthly meetings. 
===PAGE 15===
o The 100 Books Bucket List book club meets every first Tuesday morning. We average 9 
attendees at each meeting.  
o The Brighter Days book club meets every second Wednesday evening. There are now 10 
consistent members of that discussion group.  
➢ Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group attendance remains steady at 5-6 per week.  
➢ The monthly Board Game Brunch programs have been moderately successful. There were no 
participants in April, but 3 new people for May’s meeting. 
➢ Monthly Movie Nights have been hit or miss. The April program had 3 people in the audience, and 
one person left early dissatisfied with the selection, but we continue to learn and make better choices 
as the series goes on. There is more optimism for the next film on Friday May 30th. 
➢ After many conversations and interest surveys with our Weight Strengthening class participants, Niki 
Holtzman and I have decided to try a new format for the summer: Tuesday Tune-Up Sessions. These 
are bi-weekly mini exercise classes for former attendees and new patrons. This is a compromise 
between the weekly meetings we usually do for a six-week series in fall and winter, and the break we 
normally take in the busy summer months. We had 11 people at our first class on Tuesday May 6th. 
Virtual Program Update 
➢ These are events sponsored and managed by other libraries. By partnering with them we receive a 
dedicated link to track viewership and engagement from our patrons. There are no virtual programs 
booked in May or June, but there are several on deck for July and August that we will promote soon. 
o “Local Farms, MA - Produce, Meats, Dairy, Oh My!” was on April 7th. We had 4 people register and 
2 attend the event live. 
o “The Intersection of Climate Action and Romance Novels: A Panel Discussion” was on April 10th. 
We had 1 register but did not attend the program live. 
o “Tudor Queens and Ladies in Waiting: A Discussion with Authors Nicola Clark and Jade Scott” was 
on April 12th. We had 2 people register, and no one attended live. 
o “The Two Nerdy History Girls Ride Again!” on April 14th had no registrations. 
o “No More Curse! A History of the Red Sox with David Kruh” on April 28th also had no registrations.

The  Institute  of  Museum  and  Library  Services  (IMLS)  historically  has  provided  funding  to  the  states  for  library  
services.
 
Due
 
to
 
funding
 
cuts
 
at
 
the
 
federal
 
level,
 
it
 
is
 
likely
 
we
 
will
 
see
 
a
 
$3.5

at

to

for

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   Staff Update 
May 19th, Melanie attended the annual MLA conference. Some valuable sessions included discussion 
of book challenges and legal frameworks, civic literacy post-election, and updates on the current 
challenges around IMLS funding and cuts. I was certified as a Mental Health First Aid responded at a 
workshop on May 20, and the skills learned should prove valuable in referring those in need to 
resources that will help them and in reducing stigma around mental health challenges. 
I met with Deb Bookis and Tara Bastien of the schools to discuss ongoing parterships and 
collaborations. It seems as if these efforts may result in resumption of 5th grade visits! We plan to 
share resources and support around STEM topics as well.  
Town Administration will be initiating “Tri-Board” (Select Board, FinComm, and School Board) 
meetings starting this month. Their charge will be to advise on a comprehensive fiscal plan for the 
town’s future. This group will ask for more information, earlier about FY27 needs. I will be presenting 
in August about the library’s anticipated needs. This puts a little more pressure on Lydia and I to 
complete our staffing study earlier than we thought we might. 
The Conroy Memorial Garden is about half planted. Around 20 people gathered on May 24, including 
Robin, Lydia, Susan Conroy, and family and friends. Kevin Boussom is working on the sculpture and 
anticipates delivery before the fall. 
North End Subaru has expressed their commitment to another year of support for the Month Music 
Series, and their intention to support us long-term. Both Library and Jason from North End are 
appreciative of the partnership. 
   Programs 
Mother’s Day Planting 2.0 had 38 attendees! Our Enchanted Plants program brought 16, despite 
competition with the rescheduled Cruise Night. Niki’s Weight Strengthening sessions has seen a dip 
in attendance. A few program cancellations happened this past month due to low registration 
numbers, like the Women in Jazz performance, Sew Simple Sessions, movie night, and video game 
night. 
You may not have realized it but our magnificent Children’s Librarian Debbie Laffond held her final 
storytime in May. Her retirement will come in the Fall, and she will not deliver the Fall storytime 
sessions.  
Coming up: Summer Reading! Registration is ongoing, events start 6/23, including free summer 
lunches! 
   Outreach 
We have been reaching out to potential partners for outreach services, including Arrive Lunenburg 
and Tri-Town Apartments, and Smokestack Coffee Roasters. We are assembling a Welcome to 
Lunenburg and the Library packet of resources. 
• All Vendor Fair at the T.C. Passios building - Saturday, May 10th, 11:00-6:00 
===PAGE 6===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
o Weather started rainy, and ended windy, but this was a positive event with solid 
attendance. Robin manned the booth with Melody from the Friends and were joined 
by Melanie and Joslyn from the staff. Chatted with about 15 people, about half were 
not regular library users.  
• Lunenburg Cruise Night at T.C Passios Wednesday May 21   POSTPONED

▪ Building & Maintenance 
Our HVAC system has been blasting cold air lately. Seems ready for summer! Facilities helped protect 
the water feature pump from accidental damage, and the water has been running for two weeks 
now! The projector screen may be damaged, we are waiting on an assessment from an expert.  
▪ Technology 
One Verkada camera failed, and it has been replaced. 3D printing as a public service should be ready 
at some point soon in the next few months. We are really just waiting for an opportunity to get more 
than one person trained on the machines and software. We have purchased a number of Raspberry 
Pi devices to support a coding club that is planned for the Fall.

MONTHLY BUDGET REPORT:  
https://tinyurl.com/ydrjvra8 
 
HIGHLIGHTS FOR MAY: 
Story times, book clubs, Legos at the Library,& drop-in crafts all are put on pause by mid-May.  
We use the last few weeks to gear up for Summer Reading 2025 “20,00 Reads under the Sea” 
making sure we have everything ready and organized for all our wonderful upcoming events! 
 STORY TIME WITH LOCAL AUTHOR STEPHANIE LUCIER 
Local, beloved elementary teacher Stephanie Lucier came by to share her story based on her 
cat Binx: Lucky believes that he is the unluckiest cat in the world. He has tried every good luck 
charm he can think of and still he finds himself involved in silly mishap after mishap.  But with 
the help of his mom and positive thinking, Lucky perseveres and learns that luck is something 
you get to make for yourself! 
Ms. Lucier teaches at the Lunenburg Primary School and has a passion for writing. She 
currently resides in Massachusetts, with her family and her own little "Lucky" named Binx. The 
children were very excited to have their teacher as a guest reader and really enjoyed the story 
and accompanying craft. 

===PAGE 8===
          
 
MOTHER’S DAY PLANTING 
The kids who had planted seeds in April came back to the library to repot them into larger 
containers, after decorating them for someone special, to give away for Mother’s Day.  They 
were quite impressed to see how the seeds had grown into small plants!  We all worked 
diligently to make sure the seedlings survived and were happy to send them on to their new 
homes! 
CRUISE NIGHT  
By delaying this event for 1 week, we were able to enjoy a beautiful evening outside on the 
green space at T C Passios for Cruise Night 2025.  The library sponsored a nature scavenger 
hunt- with bubbles and lollipops as prizes and shared lots of information about our upcoming 
Summer Reading Program. I also assisted at the Scout table where we offered the kids a 
chance to make a wind powered balloon car- a very popular craft! All of this was accompanied 
by millions of bubbles produced by the library's bubble machine! 
                                 

===PAGE 9===
 
SENSORY STORY TIME WITH MISS PAM 
Pam Wockenfuss presented another of her delightful sensory story time programs with the 
kids and families.  This month's story was Sam’s Sandwich by David Pelham.  After reading this 
fun book, Pam gave the children time to experience the story with all of their senses as each was 
presented with a plastic box, which they were allowed to keep, containing many elements from the 
story.

Other news/updates 
- School Visits: I visited two 6th grade classes at the school library in order to promote 
summer reading. Both visits went well, and I was also able to promote some of the 
summer events as well. Pam (the school librarian) also passed along Summer 
Reading info to the ELA teachers for the 7th & 8th grade classes.  
- Teen Summer Reading: We currently have 13 teens signed up on beanstack (as of 
6/9). The first of the summer reading events was the end of school henna and that 
was a successful program with 18 total participants. Mandy from 
WickedGoodHenna is great and always draws a crowd.  
- SHAPIRO group: I met with the director of the SHAPIRO group to discuss the teen 
summer visits. They will come four days a week, Mon-Thurs, at 1pm. I will have 
activities for them to participate in throughout the week, including lego challenges, 
coloring, board games, and a craft activity one day a week. The young adult group 
might come as well for some classes such as how to apply and interview for jobs 
etc.  
- Homeschool Craft Corner/Meet-Up: These events are over for the summer but will 
be back in the fall. Overall they were regularly attended by 15 – 20 individuals 
===PAGE 12===
consistently, which was about 6 families. Attendance did drop off in the late spring 
once the weather turned nice.  
- Professional Development: I attended a three-week course on Youth Engagement 
that was hosted by the Library Journal. It was a thorough course with lots of good 
ideas and suggestions.  
o I also took a short course on how to successfully support coworkers during 
conflicts with patrons.  
- The TAB group met on Saturday, June 7th in order to put together the Summer take 
and make kits. At our last meeting we decided to change our meeting times to a 
little earlier since three of our members are also pages at the library in order to 
better accommodate their work schedules. We will also be meeting through out the 
summer.  
===PAGE 13===
Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
June 2025 
Summary of Last Month’s Programs & Events 
➢ On Saturday May 1st we had 12 people join us for the final session of the music clinic series. Due to 
their success, we will negotiate continuing the series in the fall of this year.  
➢ We hosted a fabulous Floral Design Workshop on Tuesday, May 13th. There were 15 people in 
attendance, and it was a very engaging group that made the workshop a huge success! 
➢ The annual Spring Puzzle Swap that took place on Saturday, May 17th was another successful swap 
event. We had 52 people visit to exchange 472 tickets for selections from the 656 donated puzzles. 
➢ Wednesday May 21st we postponed our Monthly Music Series program with jazz flutist Galen Abdur-
Razzaq on Zoom for a tribute to women in jazz performance. We will most likely reschedule in late 
winter or early spring when virtual programs may be more appealing. 
➢ On Friday May 23rd, we hosted an opening reception for the art exhibit: Lunenburg High School AP & 
Honors Art Thesis Show. That was very well attended, with an estimated 58 people visiting the 
reception. The exhibit ran May 17th-31st and is something we will continue to do with the school in the 
coming years. 
➢ The library sponsored author Varla Ventura for a unique talk on botanical folklore based on 
information from her book Enchanted Plants on Wednesday May 28th. There were 16 people at this 
informative and engaging talk, despite the draw of Cruise Night happening concurrently outside.  
➢ On Thursday May 29th the folk-rock duo Creeque Alley returned to perform. One of our most popular, 
well-received, and enthusiastic performers, we were very excited to welcome them back! There were 
25 people enjoying the music and singing along that evening. 
This Month’s Programs & Events 
➢  Local Lunenburg resident and personal crisis coach Pamela Dussault visited on June 4th for a talk on 
managing caregiver burnout. There were 11 people here for the program and it was very well 
received, with calls for more support groups and workshops to be held at the library in the future. 
➢ On Thursday June 12th we will host a return visit with Mark & Raianne for a folk duo concert as part of 
the Monthly Music Series. 
➢ There will be a summer puzzle race on June 17th. 
➢ For a creative workshop, the library will be holding a fused glass pendant making class on June 18th. 
➢ And as part of summer reading, on June 25th there will be a “bad art” night for patrons of all ages to 
come to the library and make creative artwork using odds and ends pieces from the craft supply. It 
should be a fun event to mark the beginning of summer! 
  
===PAGE 14===
Recurring Program Update 
➢ Book clubs are doing well with excellent discussions at our monthly meetings. 
o The 100 Books Bucket List book club meets every first Tuesday morning. We average 9 
attendees at each meeting.  
o The Brighter Days book club met every second Wednesday evening. It is being rebranded 
as Between the Bookends book club, where we will read through suggestions from our 
regular readers instead of working on a set theme for the year. There are now 10 
consistent members of that discussion group.  
➢ Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group attendance remains steady at 5-6 per week.  
➢ The monthly Board Game Brunch programs have been moderately successful. There were no 
participants in April, but 3 new people for May’s meeting. 
➢ Monthly Movie Nights have been dragging with the weather change for spring. The May program had 
no one attend. We may take a break for the summertime and bring it back for the cooler weather. 
➢ The Tuesday Tune-Up Sessions with Niki Holtzman started strong but have already tapered off. 
These are bi-weekly mini exercise classes for former attendees and new patrons. This is a 
compromise between the weekly meetings we usually do for a six-week series in fall and winter, and 
the break we normally take in the busy summer months. We had 11 people in our first class on 
Tuesday May 6th, canceled the May 20th session, and only had 3 at our June 3rd meeting. 
Virtual Program Update 
➢ There are no virtual programs booked in May or June, but there are several on deck for July and 
August that we will promote soon.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   Staff Update 
We are coming off the best statistical Summer ever for the library! Our SRP was a wild success, with 
more than 400 participants. Starting registrations at the beginning of June also supported the 
readers who were interested in starting as soon as possible. The majority of registrants signed up 
during the month of June. We also served more than 1600 lunches during SRP, thanks to Main Street 
Bank and the Friends! The summer was very busy and staff have been putting in the extra effort to 
make it a spectacular experience for all who visit the library! 
FY25 concluded with very positive statistics on the whole. Submitting ARIS in August, we counted 
68,459 visitors between July 1 and June 30. 77,543 physical items circulated (for a value of $1.148M), 
with another 34,363 digital circulations. These are all increases, with the digital circulations jumping 
almost 30% over FY24. 
The next two months promise to be quite busy for our staff. Debbie Lafond’s retirement will be in 
early October. The day for the public to visit her and express their appreciation will be October 6 
between 10 and 5. Our celebrations of the 20th Anniversary of opening this facility take place during 
October. 
We are engaged in two job searches, Children’s Librarian and a Library Page. We are about to hire for 
the Library Page position, and we will begin to invite applicants to interview for the Children’s 
Librarian position late this week or early next week. The Town Manager organized a town employee 
wide event in August, which continued to support a friendly, collaborative atmosphere for town 
employees. The Library’s own Marabeth Balboni won recognition for her excellence. 
I presented to the Tri-Board (Selectboard, School Board, Finance Committee) meeting about Library 
budget anticipated needs for FY27 and beyond on August 26. The presentation was much more 
detailed than my usual presentations and much earlier in the year. I also participated in the 
Massachusetts Library Aid Association scholarship selection process, although I was not able to 
attend in person this year. 
We are supporting significant effort to engage in professional development for both Joslyn and 
Kaitee. They have signed up for a number of courses and workshops over the summer and into the 
fall. Joslyn has worked really hard to develop a story-time calendar for September-December and will 
be delivering those story-times. This endeavor will be a hand-on learning experience to help her 
develop her Children’s Librarian muscles and to help the organization bridge a transition to a new 
Children’s Librarian. Kaitee is focusing on customer service and reader’s advisory skills. We are also 
attempting to rebuild our 5th grade visit program, and establish a partnership with the school 
librarian at the Middle-High School. So far, after school crowds are even bigger than last year! 
I will be targeting two grants this fall. One will be from Emerson Hospital and the other will be a 
Community Foundation grant. We will also be introducing at-home delivery for Meals on Wheels 
participants. If those home-bound individuals would like personally selected library materials, they 
can join our program and the books will be delivered and returned by the Meals on Wheels drivers.  
   Programs 
===PAGE 7===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
FY25 library programs brought in nearly 7,000 attendees at 410 programs. We hope that this upward 
trend will continue as we focus this year on balancing staff time with engagement at events. 
Especially with the Children’s Room transition and other anticipated commitments for our wonderful 
staff, we will have fewer resources to direct toward programming, so this balance is very important. 
After a brief lull in activity during August, the school year has really blasted off to a quick start. Book 
groups have grown (11 for both Bookworms and In-Betweeners groups), and our first story time had 
solid turn out (30) for a musical theme.  We are endeavoring to plan and schedule programs a bit 
further out to help with our workflow and conscientiously selecting events to offer. Our first Paint 
Night was a booming success: with only 20 available spots, more than 30 people were wait listed. 
In our Teen Room, we are also offering an activity (less structured and more “come and go” 
atmosphere than a “program”) three days each week. We are also offering approximately one teen 
program per week with an immediate goal of increasing program attendance. Our focus here is to 
develop an expectation around activity and engagement being regularly available for teens, an 
expectation that has been missing for a few years. 
Coming up: Around the World Storytime (9/17), Teen Coding Club (starts 9/16), LBA Lunch and Learn 
with Jay’s Cryptic Creatures (snakes, 9/20), a rebranded “After Hours” video game night (9/26), 
Monthly Music Series: History and Legends through Song with Sultana (9/24), “Stamped” Discussion 
and Author Q&A (9/25), Flu Clinic (9/20), and Weight Strengthening with Niki (returns 9/30). 
Anniversary Celebrations will be an Opening Ceremony on 10/3 and Closing Event with concert on 
10/24. Scavenger Hunts for three different age groups will be offered as well. 
   Outreach 
Outreach has been moderately successful over the summer. Having the Friends participate makes a 
lot of sense. Moving into the Fall, we are targeting events like LBA’s Trunk or Treat and visits to 
Arrive and Tri-Town. These are data gathering engagements for us, and could be very useful as a 
chance to advertise Friends benefits. Smokestack Coffee Roasters is our next target for partnership. 
We will also be starting at-home delivery partnering with LAAC’s Meals on Wheels as mentioned 
earlier in the report. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
Electricity costs are significantly higher this summer than in past years, perhaps a result of a fully 
functioning HVAC system. DPW patched our parking lot, and I have discussed with Facilities our 
future capital needs, including repairing sidewalks. 
Two Friends funded projects are in the works for the bidding process: Sail shade for patio and 
motorized shades in the Main Hall. Order submitted to Blockhouse for café furniture. 
Our projector shade motor was repaired early in the summer, and our projector cart is currently 
receiving some maintenance on its caster wheels. I tightened the wobbly tabletops in the 
Community Room and all seem to be functioning appropriately at the moment. 
===PAGE 8===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
The sculpture for the Robert Conroy Memorial Garden has been delivered. It is even more inspiring 
and heart-warming than expected. A fantastic result from Kevin Boussom, the artist. 
The rainwater garden design is complete. The DPW and its contractor should be installing the design 
this Fall. This project will add a significant new demo space to the property and support education 
around dealing with storm water runoff for homeowners and for businesses. 
▪ Technology 
As part of our preparation for the Tri-Board presentation, I met with IT department (Casey, Mason, 
Kara) to discuss our future replacement schedules and needs. Our Technology plan and replacement 
schedule should be a part of that department’s presentations moving forward. 
It does seem that our ongoing hard drive issues in our public access computers are a result of 
improper selection of hardware. Deep Freeze is likely frying the hard drives, and we may need to 
upgrade to Enterprise quality SSDs to avoid the issue. We upgraded to cloud-based management of 
Deep Freeze to save significant time for Melanie.
