HIGHLIGHTS FOR JUNE, JULY & AUGUST: 
• Our Summer Reading Program this year was a great success!  As seen above, 
we increased our numbers in all areas of registration and program attendance.  
We had 17 reader reward prizes- 90% of the items culled from donations!  It's so 
rewarding to have kids and families embrace and engage in the many activities 
that we have worked hard to put together! 
• Starting summer reading registration on June 1st proved to be a good decision.  
We were able to capture and inform more young families during a time when it 
was not as busy here in the library. 
• Joslyn did an excellent job planning and executing the weekly craft sessions for 
the kids. There was lots of enthusiastic participation in these events! 
• Weekly lunch and story time started off slowly but definitely gained momentum 
as we went along.  Offering a storytime event in the summer was a request that 
we have had many times, and it felt appropriate to offer this service. Sitting in the 
air-conditioned Story Craft Room, eating your delicious, free lunch while listening 
to a story or two definitely worked for many families. 
• Our Ice Cream Social was a big hit!  We provided the ice cream and invited 
families to bring their favorite toppings.  We had a well-organized set-up and lots 
of volunteer help which certainly contributed to the success of this event- and 
who doesn’t love a bowl of yummy ice cream shared with friends on a steamy, 
hot day! 
• We hosted a return favorite event this summer- Water Day. Using a couple of 
hoses, some buckets and sponges, a jar of marbles, and, of course, our lovely 
water feature- the kids were able to play with water in so many fun ways!  This 
fun event cost $0, required lots of sweat equity and help from an army of 
volunteers!   Kids and water- we couldn’t miss! 
• A visit with the Whale Mobile was also a star event of the summer.  Sitting inside 
a 40’ whale while someone educates and entertains you about these amazing 
creatures is an experience that the kids will remember for a long time! 
===PAGE 11===
• Another big hit of the summer was an alpaca story time with Kristi Mahoney.  
Ms Mahoney shared her book Alpacas Make Terrible Librarians (it was agreed 
that they do) and then we all got to visit with and learn about Jackie Chabot’s 
alpacas! 
• Kids and families got to meet Dr Fortuna, our new superintendent, here at the 
library.  Dr. Fortuna hosted a lively story time where she shared a couple of 
books about starting school and answered questions from the kids and families.  
She was wonderful- calm, relaxed and friendly! 
• On a warm, sunny Friday afternoon, kids and families gathered at the DPW 
building to “touch a truck” So many participants for this great event!  We were a 
presence at this and shared, along with information about our fall activities at the 
library, temporary truck tattoos, lollipops and lots of goodwill.  We are hoping this 
becomes an annual event! 
• And, lastly, a huge thank you to Main Street Bank for providing the opportunity to 
serve free lunches every Monday-Thursday. Many families came by every day 
to take advantage of this, and we know that for many of these it was fulfilling a 
need.  It felt great to be able to do this for our patrons.    
 
 
 
 
UPCOMING EVENTS FOR SEPTEMBER:

• September 8-29th: Sounds and Music Storytime for 0-3's 
• September 10-24th- Around the World Storytime for 3’s,4’s 5’s

Respectfully submitted:

Joslyn Davis, Library Assistant, Children’s Area

Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
September 2025 
 
Summary of Last Month 
➢ This year we had a very busy summer reading program for adults! 
o The summer reading program ran from June 1st - August 2nd  
o There were 74 readers who logged 164,016 minutes on Beanstack 
o We held 7 adult events during the program with a total of 159 attendees 
➢ After the summer reading program ended, there were still some successful events 
in the month of August. 
o Our puzzle events went well with 14 people at our summer puzzle race, and 
56 people stopping by for the summer puzzle swap.  
o Our music program for the month was a performance by Didgeridoo Down 
Under, who did a fantastic job educating our audience about Australian 
ecology, animals, culture and folklore during a very impressive show. The 
timing was not ideal, but there were still 16 people in attendance who really 
enjoyed the concert.  
Recurring Program Updates 
➢ Book club attendance dipped a bit with inconsistent summer schedules, but we 
anticipate getting back to full participation for fall and winter meetings.  
o 100 Books Bucket List is discussing The Power Broker by Robert Caro at the 
next meeting on Tuesday October 7th 
o Between the Bookends is discussing Blowing the Bloody Doors Off by 
Michael Caine at the next meeting on Wednesday October 8th 
➢ Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group also continues growing! We see a minimum of 
6 people every Tuesday for the sewing group with a few new faces every so often.  
➢ Drop-in tech help with Melanie sees intermittent participation, as expected, but 
many who stop by find it helpful to have a set time for their technology questions. 
This is an easy program to continue, and we hope to see patron engagement grow 
with time. Right now, this program runs bi-weekly on Saturday mornings when 
Melanie is scheduled to be in the library.

 ○  The  requester  did  not  attend  this  meeting.  ○  The  book  was  made  available  for  the  Trustees  to  read.  ○  Muir  gathered  professional  reviews  of  the  book  for  the  Trustees  to  read.  ○  Harry  read  us  the  emails  that  he  and  Muir  received  from  the  requester  about  this  matter.  ○  Muir  said  this  book  is  in  more  than  half  of  CWMARS  libraries  and  most  shelve  it  in  their  picture  book  sections.  ○  David  said  that  shelving  it  in  the  parenting  section  can  seem  to  imply  that  the  subject  matter  is  not  for  children  and

○  We  reviewed  library  policies  relating  to  this  matter.  Our  Child  Safety  Policy  states  that,  “Children  under  the  age  of

(12)

by

and

to

“Recognizing  that  any  community  contains  people  with  different  backgrounds,  sensibilities,  
personalities
 
and

must

to

that

all

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   FY25 Budget Update 
Payment for computer hardware submitted, but not on the books yet. 
   FY26 Budget Update  
 
   Staff Update 
We have hired a new Children’s Librarian. Joslyn Davis has been our Library Assistant in the Children’s 
Room for the last 3 years almost, and has had the opportunity to learn and experience many tasks 
under Debbie’s guidance. She has shown remarkable growth during her time thus far, and I expect 
that she will continue to grow. Her passion and dedication to excellence should help her achieve 
wondrous things for the library in support of our community. We have begun our search to fill the 
now vacant Library Assistant position. 
 Lydia and I are working on a Policy and Procedure handbook for staff to assist them in their regular 
interactions. We have introduced a number of more stringent controls to help support staff during 
challenging situations, and this handbook will help ensure we approach situations with consistency. 
She and I are also in preparations for the parental leaves that are on the near horizon. I am hoping to 
meet with the Town Manager to further discuss these plans in the next few days. 
I am working with Pauline and Deb at LAAC to submit our Emerson community grant. We will also 
submit a Bemis Community Fund Grant application. Both are due November 7.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
The Town’s Financial Department has been decimated lately, two resignations and a retirement, so 
payments are a bit behind. The Town Manager has made every effort to smooth payroll, but she 
does anticipate a few hiccups while payroll is being processed by a consultant. 
   Programs 
Joslyn’s Monday Around the World Storytimes have been SUPER popular, 4 session with 124 
attendees so far! Books clubs continue to gather steady interest. Tween D&D has been steady, and 
the last few concerts in our Monthly Music Series have been fantastic in quality and high in 
attendance.  
We are entering a transitional period with fewer staff available temporarily and then three parental 
leaves. The frequency of programming will drop slightly, even though we are making arrangements 
to keep our momentum carrying forward. 
Coming up: DIY Halloween Masks for Tweens/Teens 10/16, Homeschool Craft Corner 10/20, Tween 
D&D 10/22, “Murder in Rockport” Book Talk with Authors 10/22, Anniversary Closing Ceremony and 
Concert 10/24, Tech Help Drop-In with Melanie 10/25, Storytime Costume Parade 10/27, Book 
Bedazzling for Adults 10/29, Wildcrafted Apothecary Workshop 11/1, Book Sale 11/7+8, Small Works 
Exhibit Opens 11/15 
   Outreach 
Upcoming outreach: Shrieks and Krieks with The Club 10/18 (3+ staff), Literacy Night at the Primary 
School 10/22 (2 staff), LBA’s Trunk or Treat 10/25 (2 staff), 10/28 Outreach to Baker Creek apartments. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
The building HVAC has been receiving attention as we prepare to transition to heating season.  
The rainwater garden has been planted, with one small snafu that has been remedied. Still waiting 
on the curb cuts, but native grasses are already germinating! We are making preparations for Spring 
to removed the juniper ground cover in front of the Community Wing to make way for more native 
plantings. 
▪ Technology 
Our Staff Color printer will be getting replaced in the next few days. This device is one of the oldest 
pieces of hardware in the building and has served us well. The IT department had a barely used 
surplus printer that should work for us. Town-wide, we are reducing the number of standalone 
printers to streamline costs. The library currently has 5 printers in addition to the copy machine.

HIGHLIGHTS FOR SEPTEMBER: 
• Story times have resumed!  We’re getting lots of enthusiastic participation and 
many new families.  Joslyn has a well-developed curriculum planned for the two 
different age groups.  For the littlest participants we have Sounds and Music 
Storytime: “As little ones start to become more aware of their surroundings, they 
are discovering so many different worlds of sounds. To help them along their 
noisy adventure, we’ll be examining a new sound theme every week. Depending 
on the week, there could be everything from interactive stories, silly sounds, 
instrument play, dancing, crafts and more!” 
     For the older 3-5 group, the theme is Around the World Storytime: a whimsical 
journey across the globe as we explore vibrant cultures, places, and holidays 
from around the world. Each week, we’ll set our sights on a new exciting holiday 
or location - discovering captivating tales and festivities through stories, music, 
activities, and crafts! 
• We have 2 new book clubs this year.  Joslyn has developed a year of book 
sharing, The Beginners Book Club, for kids ages 6-8 where they will have an 
opportunity to: chat about our latest book, share our favorite moments, discover new 
perspectives, eat some yummy treats/snacks, and do crafts/activities that bring our 
books to life! It’s a fantastic way for your 1st or 2nd grader to make new friends and 
discover a love of reading!  
===PAGE 8===
     The second book club, The Bookworms, is designed to engage kids ages 8-11 
who are middle grade readers looking for a place to find new stories and talk 
about them!   
                      Many reading adventures ahead!

Respectfully submitted: 
Debbie Laffond, Children’s Services Librarian   
Joslyn Davis, Library Assistant, Children’s Area     
 
 
 
Dear Muir and Board of Trustees, 
Thank you so much for giving me the opportunity to serve as the Children’s Librarian these past ten 
years. It has been such a joy to share stories, laughter, and learning with our community’s children. I’m 
truly grateful for your trust and support.  I have always felt the respect and gratitude that you have 
extended to me as I went along this journey.   
Martha asked me what my goals were as the Children’s Librarian here at LPL.  My number one objective 
was to help create an environment that made people want to, not just drive by and admire this beautiful 
building, but to pull into the parking lot and come on in. Mission accomplished, I'd say! 
Thanks for giving me my dream job and for “having my back” all these years! 
With appreciation, 
Miss Debbie

===PAGE 10===
- After-school activities have been going well with an average of 2 participants per 
day. There are now snacks available for the participants so there has been a slight 
bump in participation thanks to that. These activities have also attracted older 
teens, from 10th to 11th grade, which is usually an age we don’t see participating in 
teen activities at the library much. 
- TAB events have slowed down for the fall. Most of the TAB members are usually 
engaged in sports during this time of year and don’t have time for much else. I 
believe that TAB activity will pick back next month when most of the sports they play 
end.  
- Program participation has averaged at 5 participants per program for the past two 
months. Video game night and tween dungeons & dragons have so far had the most 
engagement. The tweens at dungeons & dragons are very excited to have a game to 
play and several of the parents have told me that they are grateful that Lunenburg is 
holding such an event regularly.  
===PAGE 11===
Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
October 2025 
 
Summary of Last Month 
➢ To start the fall season, we had a return to normal levels of programming, with some 
great successes marking the month of September. 
o Our monthly music concert was on September 4th by a regular performer, 
Dan Kirouac, who has been here a few times with his band Creeque Alley. 
This concert featured the music of Paul McCartney and was one of our best 
attended performances this year with 70 people in the audience. 
o Another success was a Friday Night Paint Night with local artist Tina Senecal 
on September 12th. We had 22 painters attend and a waitlist that could have 
filled another class; a big hit for us! 
o We also participated in the Mass Center for the Book’s statewide read of the 
graphic adaptation of Stamped from the Beginning: A History of Racist Ideas 
in America this month, with a book discussion on September 15th attended 
by 7 people, followed by a virtual interview with the illustrator on September 
25th that drew 5 more people. It was a very engaged group with an excellent 
civil discussion at both events. 
o At the end of the month, we started our annual fall Weight Strengthening 
series with Niki Holtzman on September 30th. This 5-week series has 16 
registrants with many new faces in attendance. 
Recurring Program Updates 
➢ Each book club had 6 participants in September, but we anticipate more in the fall.  
o 100 Books Bucket List is discussing The Catcher in the Rye by J.D. Salinger at 
the next meeting on Tuesday November 4th 
o Between the Bookends is discussing Still Alice by Lisa Genova at the next 
meeting on Wednesday November 12th 
➢ The Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group also continues to grow! We saw 8 people 
every Tuesday for the sewing group with a few new faces every so often.  
➢ Drop-in tech help with Melanie sees intermittent participation, as expected, but 
many find it helpful to have a set time for their technology questions. This is an easy 
program to continue, and we hope to see patron engagement grow with time. Right 
===PAGE 12===
now, this program runs bi-weekly on Saturday mornings when Melanie is scheduled 
to be in the library.

Admin Report 
Due to my upcoming parental leave in the spring, Muir and I have been working on 
additional admin/planning projects to assist the library in my temporary absence. Among 
these projects include a best practice procedural manual for uncommon situations that 
may arise in the library, updated documentation around tasks like scheduling, payroll, and 
financial processing, and sketching out potential delegation of responsibilities on staff.  
These are ongoing projects between myself and the Director to ensure there is adequate 
planning and coverage before I am unavailable in April. 
 
Respectfully submitted, 
===PAGE 14===
Lydia Paolilli, Assistant Library Director

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   Staff Update 
We are currently interviewing candidates for our open Library Assistant position in the Children’s 
Room. Our Teen Librarian Kaitee has resigned, and the job has been posted last Thursday. Although 
we are now at a critically low staffing level, with two parental leaves coming up, we can move to fill 
our open positions.   
Lydia and I have met and discussed with the Town Manager and the Assistant Town Manager/HR 
Director the duties Lydia will perform while I am on leave. They are in negotiations with the Union 
about a stipend to compensate Lydia for this additional responsibility. 
I submitted two grant applications in early November. One for $1000 from the Bemis Community 
Fund for five monthly sessions of music and movement storytime with Monument Square 
Community Music School. The other grant was from the Emerson Hospital Catherine Gallery 
Community Grant for materials to support the LAAC’s Memory Café program. There will be 10 
memory kits that can circulated to Alzheimer sufferers and their caregivers. These will be housed at 
the LAAC and catalogued by the library. 
The Town’s Financial Department continues its transition. The Finance Director who was hired has 
decided not to stay, and payments will continue to be slow. Two consultants have been tasked to 
help, one with payroll and one with Accounts Payable. An Assistant Accountant has been hired. 
STM has a significant update to the SAP. 
   Programs 
The biggest update in Programming is our Teen calendar. We have had to cancel a number of 
programs, though we are continuing what we can. One of the two book clubs will likely not continue, 
but we have serendipitously had a volunteer paraprofessional from the High School who will try to 
get a board game club off the ground on Thursday afternoons! She is learning about DM’img D&D 
campaigns and may be able to take over leading that series. Our Teen CPR program has a number of 
registrations despite being scheduled the weekend after Thanksgiving.  
Our Anniversary celebration was a big success and the Friends seemed very happy with the result. 
We have continued to offer the Library of Services fundraiser. Our Small Works 2025 exhibit in 
hanging and we had almost 30 attendees at the opening. The Wildcrafted Apothecary Event was also 
remarkably successful with 25 attendees. This year’s Fall Puzzle Swap had 56 attendees, but more 
than 760 puzzles were dropped off (a record high). Our bluegrass duo drew 51 attendees, and our 
weekly Stitchcraft group has create more than 50 hats to donate to the local Meaghan’s Hats charity! 
Holiday programs are about to hit. The Winter Artisan showcase will be December 7th. 40 vendors 
signed up within minutes of the registration being opened. 10 vendors will provide an indoor 
“Farmer’s Market” in the Teen Room during the event. “The Dirty Martinis” will perform afterward. 
They have inquired about continuing a regular market during the winter season, too. The First 
Thursday of December will be our annual craft to follow the tree lighting in the Town center. And 
December 13th will be the family gingerbread house event that has been popular the last few years. 
===PAGE 5===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
Coming up: 11/20 Board Game Club, 11/29 CPR for Teens, 11/22 The Dangerous Art of Chainsaw 
Sculpture, 12/4 DIY Ugly Sweaters, 12/7 Winter Showcase, 12/13 Gingerbread Houses. 
   Outreach 
Our presentation at Baker Creek received very positive review and Melanie was invited back to talk 
to them again. She had 17 interested and curious residents in attendance on 10/28. Trunk or Treat 
was AMAZING. We saw several hundred children and families, and this event with the LBA is 
definitely something we should do every year. Similarly, Literacy Night was a great event to be a part 
of with about 50 interactions. Most were library users we know very well, we did sign up 8 people for 
library cards. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
The furniture purchased by the Friends for our Café has arrived and looks fantastic. On the whole, it 
brightens and opens up our café area. We are still tuning in the HVAC settings to get the temps in the 
range we want. 
▪ Technology 
Our Staff Color printer was replaced and now resides in the Children’s Room Office to make labels 
for cataloging. The new printer is fantastic, so far. 
CWMARS has suggested that all libraries with a fiber line work towards CIPA compliance. With the 
developments over the years with filtering software, this seems like a reasonable course to enable 
some significant discounts.

===PAGE 7===
Highlights:  
LBA TRUNK OR TREAT: Muir and I (with the help of Sienne, Wren, Torben, and Maisie) were 
able to interact with hundreds of Lunenburg folks. While there were a lot of familiar faces, we 
also had plenty of new interests among families.  
PRESCHOOL STORYTIME: Despite losing the early learning center kids due to their relocation, 
there’s been a steady growth of attendees in our Around the World Preschool storytime. We 
started at 10 and are now getting around 22 each time! 
 
 
CREEPY CRAWLIES: The Joys of Nature returned for a spooky live animal program and brought 
some extra scary snakes (at least to me) and an adorable tarantula – much to the delight of the 
kids. 
   
 
BOOKWORMS BOOK CLUB: Five kids might not seem like a lot, but to have a steady kid’s book 
club of enthusiastic and friendly voices is truly a monthly delight. 
 
 
HALLOWEEN COSTUME PARADE: Our annual tradition was yet again a big hit! We had perfect 
weather, a generous police escort for safety, tons of cute costumes (from the kids and town 
helpers alike), and (of course) a LOT of candy. 

===PAGE 8===
    
WAMPANOAG POTTERY: Well-attended, informative, and hands-on! Tim from Plimoth Patuxet 
Museums was great with the kids and made sure they would all go home with something they 
were proud of! We received many comments from parents praising the program – both for its 
execution and its uniqueness.

Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
November 2025 
 
Summary of Last Month 
➢ The main highlight of October was a fantastic showing of support and engagement 
for our Library Building’s 20th Anniversary celebrations. Additionally, we had some 
great single programs to round out a successful month of events at the library! 
o On October 3rd we had a welcoming event for the month-long celebration of 
the library building being 20 years old. This event included remarks from 
committee members, Friends, and the Director, as well as a presentation by 
Bill Tyler of the historical society on the history of libraries in town. Donuts 
was donated by Hollis Hills Farm and cider was donated by Lanni Orchards. 
There were an estimated 50 people at this opening event. 
o Our monthly music concert was on October 9th by bluegrass duo Flynn 
Cohen and Laura Orshaw. This stop was part of their local tour for bluegrass 
concerts, and we drew an impressive 51 people for their show here. 
o Two authors of a true crime book based in Rockport came on October 22nd to 
discuss their book and sell copies. We had 8 very engaged attendees come 
for a cozy book talk that evening. 
o At the end of the month, we closed the anniversary celebrations with two 
events: a family-oriented “birthday party” for the library with a balloon 
twister, face painting and temporary tattoos; and a jazzukulele performance 
sponsored by the local community council. Both were major successes with 
a combined estimated 75 people at the library for the evening. 
Recurring Program Updates 
➢ Each book club had 8 participants in October.  
o 100 Books Bucket List is discussing The Corrections by Jonathan Franzen at 
the next meeting on Tuesday December 2nd  
o Between the Bookends is discussing The Winter Solstice by Rosamunde 
Pilcher at the next meeting on Wednesday December 10th 
➢ The Stitchcraft knitting and crochet group also remains steady. The group worked 
on making hats and winterwear for a charity group called Meaghan’s Hats that 
donates materials to schools, shelters and unhoused individuals in Central 
===PAGE 10===
Massachusetts. 
 
 
➢ Drop-in tech help with Melanie sees intermittent participation, as we expected, but 
many find it helpful to have a set time for their technology questions. This is an easy 
program to continue, and we hope to see patron engagement grow with time. Right 
now, this program runs bi-weekly on Saturday mornings when Melanie is scheduled 
to be in the library. 
  

===PAGE 11===
November Programs 
➢ The wildcrafted apothecary workshop with Grace Krupkowski was held on Saturday, 
November 1st. 
➢ On Wednesday, November 5th, we held a fall puzzle race. 
➢ We partnered with the Association of Financial Planners for a talk  
➢ The annual fall puzzle swap followed the puzzle race on Saturday, November 15th. 
➢ Our monthly music series concert for November will be on Wednesday, November 
19th when the Asphodel Pagan Choir performs. 
➢ Wrapping up the month will be a talk with a chainsaw artist on Saturday, November 
22nd where he will explain his journey in the field of chainsaw artistry.

===PAGE 3===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
 
   
 
 
   Staff Update 
Muir and Sienne welcomed a baby boy on Thursday, December 18th. I have not received official notice 
of his leave starting yet, but it is understood by myself and the Town Manager, Jen Warren-Dyment, 
to be underway in his continued absence.  
An offer letter has been extended to Minna Scholten for the Library Assistant – Children's Room 
position. She has accepted and start dates are being negotiated. Minna may work some Fridays in 
January for initial training but is anticipating availability to accept the full schedule for the position 
during the week of January 26th. We are excited to welcome her to our team! 
A second offer letter for temporary employment has also been extended to Debra Laffond to start 
immediately. She will work less than 20 hours per week as a regular schedule, and will be hired as a 
SAP (salary admin plan) Staff Librarian. Her assistance will be invaluable in covering desk shifts and 
providing much needed coverage. 
The Teen Librarian search has been suspended during Muir’s leave. I anticipate that we will need to 
repost the position upon his return, as I cannot fill in for the hiring decision process during the 
interim. In the meantime, staff are prepared to address teen behavior as needed, and we have two 
community volunteers to assist with some much-needed programming. One is running a board game 
club on Wednesday afternoons and a Dungeons and Dragons club on Friday afternoons. The other 
volunteer will assist Joslyn with the homeschool craft programs. 
   Programs 
A major success for us recently was the 2nd annual Winter Artisan Showcase on Sunday, December 
7th. We hosted over 40 vendors and were consistently busy during the market hours of 9:00 am to 
1:00 pm. Many vendors reported record sales, and our Friends group received $1061.50 in donations 
from the bake sale and the organizer Kate Ravellette. This is sure to be an annual tradition that will 
continue to grow and improve with time! 
For adults, we held another very popular puzzle swap on November 15th with a record number of 765 
puzzles available to attendees. That same day was also our Small Works art exhibit reception, which 
kicked off the annual exhibit for 2025. There have been a few sales so far, but many more 
compliments on the beautiful artwork in the Wallace room. This is another successful exhibit for the 
library and the local artist community. 
This winter we are also partnering with the Ashland Public Library to host 11 virtual programs via 
Zoom in January and February. These are promoted through a special brochure available at the desk 
and will be included in the newsletter to gain traction with our digital audiences.  
Teen events continue to be sparse with the absence of staff in that department; however, we did 
offer a successful felting program on November 18th and a CPR training class run by a member of the 
Lunenburg Fire Department/EMS on November 29th. Joslyn has taken over one of the tween book 
clubs to keep it running in the interim, while the older teen book club is unfortunately canceled.   
===PAGE 4===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
 
   
 
In addition to the extra book club, Joslyn has been providing excellent programs for kids and families 
in the children’s department. Story time attendance remains steady for both sessions, and the two 
main programs for December were substantial hits for the library. The Family Ornament Craft on 
December 4th took place after the town tree lighting, and 76 people stopped by in the cold weather 
to warm up with hot chocolate and make their annual ornament. A few weeks later we hosted the 
family Gingerbread House program to much success, as usual. These popular programs are staples of 
holiday programming, and it’s encouraging to see the engagement continue through the years. 
Coming up: 1/6 Magical Winter Lights (a magic show for families), 1/8 & 1/22 Yoga and Breathwork 
Class, 1/10 Winter Sowing Workshops, 1/20 Teen Nautical Crafting, 1/24 Golden Oldies Sock Hop Party, 
1/30 Winter Salve Workshop, 1/31 Toddler Yoga. 
   Outreach 
We did not attend or participate in any outreach events in November; however, we did spend a good 
deal of time discussing our plans moving forward. Melanie has planned a second event at 
Smokestack Roasters on Sunday, January 18th to reprise the Library of Things petting zoo program 
during a more popular time. Jaci Nicoll, one of the co-owners, is supportive of libraries and assisting 
us in outreach. Developing this partnership is a goal for 2026.  
A follow-up event at Tri Town in February 2026 is also in the works. We want to capitalize on the 
interest we saw in October by delving deeper into some digital resources accessible with a library 
card. Melanie is preparing a presentation on Libby that should find some success with this 
demographic.  
▪ Building & Maintenance 
No changes that I am aware of to report.  
▪ Technology 
CWMARS has suggested that all libraries with a fiber line work towards CIPA compliance. With the 
developments over the years with filtering software, this seems like a reasonable course to enable 
some significant discounts. This work was completed on Thursday, December 4th.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
 
   
 
 
   Staff Update 
Due to some extended delays at the town hall, our new hire for the Library Assistant – Children's 
Room will be starting on Friday, January 16th. She will assume the full schedule for the position 
during the week of January 26th while working the next couple Fridays to learn more about the front 
desk and basic library workflow. 
Sherri and I started discussing how to best manage homebound book deliveries through the COA 
Meals on Wheels program. She and I are working on creating a system to track and manage loans 
while still balancing the patron’s right to full borrowing privileges in network. We plan to have a 
system in place by next week at the latest. 
Marabeth has almost completed another full inventory of the library’s collections. She started last 
April and works in small batches when cataloging is slow, and she has some extra time. The inventory 
helps us identify missing or lost items in the collection and was last done during the closure for 
COVID in 2020. She’s made excellent progress in this important collection task!  
I attended a town department head meeting on January 8th and communicated the budget needs to 
Muir. Our Town Manager gave a brief overview of the anticipated state of the budget for FY27, and it 
will require Muir’s input as to how it affects the library. He and I have been in communication about 
what support I can offer him in this process during his leave, but all proposed changes will ultimately 
need to come from him at this time. 
   Programs 
January got off to a slow start for adult programming, most likely due to a combination of cold 
weather and cold illnesses keeping patrons home instead of venturing out to the library. Our DIY 
“make it monthly” evening program was canceled due to the weather, with a rescheduled date TBD, 
and the morning session had low attendance but great feedback. However, our first yoga class and 
winter sowing program were big successes for the library. Thank you to Robin Venezia and her crew 
of volunteers for making another wonderful winter sowing program at the library! 
Joslyn has been booking some excellent new programs for the children’s room and is seeing a lot of 
interest in the toddler yoga sessions, in addition to the return of her drop-in crafts and the usual 
recurring events. We have been working on creating a more robust STEM offering for the spring, 
especially during the school vacation weeks, and booking more diverse programming in general. 
In partnership with art teacher Scott Barry-Grove from the middle high school, there will be a new art 
exhibit installed on January 15th running through February 12th showcasing work from all students in 
the Lunenburg schools. We are hosting a reception for artists and their families to see the exhibit on 
Thursday, February 5th.  
Coming up: 2/8 Colored Pencil Drawing class, 2/12 Copper Bookmark workshops for teens and adults, 
2/13 Silent Film Night with Live Music, 2/18 Afrobeats kids dance class, 2/21 Winter Puzzle Swap. 
===PAGE 4===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
 
   
 
▪ Outreach 
Melanie is prepared for our second event at Smokestack Roasters on Sunday, January 18th to reprise 
the Library of Things petting zoo program during a more popular time. Joslyn will be going with her 
to highlight the children’s STEM kits and be an extra set of hands for library inquiries.  
The February visit to Baker Brook/Tri-Town apartment buildings is still on. Melanie has prepared a 
presentation on Libby to give to residents. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
I communicated with Chris in the facilities department to ensure the library receives adequate snow 
clearing on weekends. We had a snowy Saturday with facilities crews arriving in the afternoon, well 
past when we opened. Luckily, Marabeth and I were able to clear sidewalks for patrons by our 10:00 
am opening, but I did stress to Chris the importance of having assistance prior to the 9:00 am arrival 
of staff. All should be fine for the remainder of the winter season, but it is something I will follow up 
on if needed.  
▪ Technology 
Our new color printer is a bit more finicky than the last one and requires more oversight than 
anticipated, but it does help to have the added features and is overall a good addition.

  Members  Absent:  None   The  LPLBT  meeting  was  called  to  order  at  6:51  p.m.  by  Chair  Harry  Kubetz.   Public  Comment  ●  Muir  thanked  us  for  completing  his  performance  evaluation  and  for  our  ongoing  support.  He  will  continue  to  strive  to  keep  up  
with
 
our

  ○  Building  &  Maintenance  Update  Bill  Tyler  has  been  helping  with  some  extra  cleaning  and  maintenance  around  the  library.  We  are  still  waiting  on  the  final  steps  of  the  electronic  lock  system.   ●  CIPA  Presentation  ○  The  Children’s  and  Internet  Protection  Act  (CIPA),  is  a  federal  law  which  permits  the  government  to  require  
entities
 
receiving
 
certain
 
kinds
 
of
 
federal
 
funding
 
to
 
filter
 
internet
 
content.
 ○  CWMARS  recently  upgraded  a  fiber-optic  switch  for  our  internet.  There  is  federal  funding  available  through  
the
 
FCC’s
 
E-Rate
 
program
 
to
 
subsidize
 
the
 
cost
 
of
 
this
 
line.
 
In
 
order
 
to
 
get
 
this
 
subsidy,
 
the
 
library
 
would
 
need
 
to
 
agree
 
to
 
install
 
filtering
 
software
 
in
 
order
 
to
 
be
 
in
 
compliance
 
with
 
CIPA.
 
Compliance
 
requires
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
filtering
 
software
 
for
 
internet
 
access.
 ○  We  do  not  currently  have  a  filter.  It  is  against  library  policy  for  people  to  access  pornography  in  the  building  or  
on
 
our
 
network.
 ○  Muir  has  submitted  forms  to  CWMARS  expressing  our  intent  to  be  in  compliance  with  CIPA  so  that  we  can  
apply
 
for
 
the
 
grants
 
to
 
subsidize
 
the
 
cost
 
of
 
the
 
line.
 
However,
 
this
 
does
 
not
 
bind
 
us.
 
We
 
can
 
change
 
our
 
mind
 
before
 
June
 
30,
 
2026.
 ○  The  Trustees  need  to  decide  if  we  want  to  install  a  filter  and  censor  information  so  that  we  can  receive  a  
discount,
 
or
 
do
 
we
 
want
 
to
 
forgo
 
the
 
discount
 
and
 
decline
 
to
 
censor
 
information.
 
The
 
discount
 
would
 
be
 
about
 
$1,500.
 ○  Questions  we  have  are:  ■  What  information  will  be  censored?  ■  Who  determines  what  will  be  censored?  ■  Will  we  get  a  list  of  websites  and  information  that  is  being  censored?  ■  Can  we  create  a  whitelist  to  prevent  certain  websites  from  being  censored?  ■  Do  we  have  liability  if  we  don’t  censor?  ■  Are  there  any  unexpected  expenses  connected  with  this  software  that  will  negate  the  value  of  the  
discount
 
we
 
receive
 
on
 
the
 
fiber
 
line?
 
 
 ○  Muir  will  meet  with  a  representative  from  Cisco,  who  will  be  providing  the  software,  to  get  answers  to  our  
questions.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
   FY26 Budget Update  
 
*updated to reflect this week’s warrant.  
   FY27 Budget Update  
March 5 will be the Library’s presentation to the Finance Committee. The Town Manager is 
anticipated to be delivering her preliminary budget this week, a level service budget. A level 
service budget for the town is anticipated to need an override. 
   Staff Update 
Lydia performed to rave reviews while I was on leave. She efficiently took care of business and was 
acknowledged at last week’s Selectboard meeting by the Town Manager. She effectively monitored 
and mentored staff while I was unavailable. Debbie Laffond is helping as a temporary employee. She 
will take some time away during March and then return to help us during Lydia’s leave. 
Minna Scholten is our new Library Assistant, and we are excited to see how she can contribute. She 
seems to have picked up familiarity with circulation duties quickly, and is helping us with small 
projects in the Children’s Room. Lydia and I are continuing regular checkins with Joslyn and Melanie 
to maintain our availability for them as they work on their projects.  
We were awarded a grant from the Emerson hospital Christine Gallery Community Benefit Grant and 
will purchase memory kits that can circulated to Alzheimer sufferers and their caregivers. These will 
be housed at the LAAC and catalogued by the library.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
Karen Barret is the new Town Finance Director, the Assistant Accountant continues. I have a meeting 
with Karen and the Town Manager on Friday of this week to discuss the preliminary budget for the 
Library. 
   Programs 
Joslyn has really gone above and beyond to organize and schedule a steady stream of teen/tween 
programs while we have had the vacancy in the Teen Room. We have also been lucky to have a 
volunteer interested in leading a weekly board game club and a Dungeons and Dragon group. We 
continue to hope that the numbers for those events will increase. Our Minecraft Tournament drew 
20 excited participants on Feb 14th. 
Children’s crafts have seen a positive response with 32 attendees for Sock Snowmen and 38 for Love 
Bugs. Toddler Yoga has been another positive note with 19 little guys in attendance this past month. 
Adult programs have seen some weather impacts, though many programs are seeing steady 
numbers. 15 attended the treasure journaling workshop, and 15 attended the sock hop concert. An 
interesting note: attendance at virtual programs this month climbed to 15 at the 5 events we 
advertised, a significant increase over recent months. Our Valentines Day movie showing brought in 
a small crowd who were thoroughly entertained last Friday night.  
Coming up: 2/18 Afrobeats Dance class for Kids, 2/18 Scottish Music concert, 2/19 STEM Olympics, 2/20 
Winter Salve workshop, 2/21 Winter Puzzle Swap, 2/27 Video Game Night, 2/28 next session of 
Toddler Yoga, 3/2 Bridie Wolejko art exhibit “Harmony and Discord,” 3/7 Music Clinic, 3/14 Candle 
Making sessions for kids and for teens. 
   Outreach 
Currently working on building a list of volunteers who might help us offer a Repair Clinic Outreach 
event. Melanie is working on scheduling a second presentation at Baker Brook, and will be visiting 
the Lunenburg Women’s Club to talk about library services at one of their two next meetings. 
We are have begun filling out our calendar for late Spring and summer. If you notice any potential 
visits, let me know. Right now, Cruise Night, May Vendor Fair, Artisan Markets, and several visits to 
local recreation spots (beach, pickleball courts, etc.) are what we are planning.  
▪ Building & Maintenance 
Bill Tyler has been helping out us with a some extra cleaning and maintenance around the library. 
He’s always a reliable and hard-working helper. We are still waiting for the final steps on the 
electronic lock system.  
▪ Technology 
We are monitoring the toner usage in the new color printer, trialing a lower “quality” setting. The old 
machine had been dialed in and used only a moderate amount of color toner, and this machine needs 
to be tuned in. The old machine also finally gave out (it limped along just long enough!) As the town 
===PAGE 6===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
continue to try to minimize extra printers, we have identified at least two that we feel very strongly 
that we need to keep for our operations: the color machine and the staff black-and-white. 
Sherri’ workstation is on the list the be replaced as soon as we can make it happen. Nothing 
catastrophic, but we delayed that replacement last year. The Friends have purchased replacement 
Early Literacy Stations in the Children’s Room with a gift in honor Anne L Rivard and Charlene M 
Wagner, supplemented with other funds from the Friends.

of smiles and giggles as always.

Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
February 2026 
 
Summary of Last Month 
➢ The yoga and breathwork classes showed a lot of initial interest with full classes 
and long waitlists for the January sessions. However, we had nearly 50% no-show 
rates for each program. We will adjust class sizes to absorb some of these 
absences, and an email was sent to all February and March registrants to be 
considerate of their registration. Hopefully we will see improvement for the 
remainder of the classes. 
➢  The treasure journaling workshop on Friday, January 16th went really well. We had 
15 enthusiastic crafters attend and create unique hand-sewn journals out of 
recycled materials. It was highly reviewed and these presenters will be on my list of 
programmers to ask back in the future for more crafting events. 
➢ On January 24th we held our Sock Hop Golden Oldies party despite the frigid 
temperatures outside. There were 15 people in the audience for this very fun and 
engaging concert. Denis Cormier brought costumes and great energy to make this 
an unforgettable show at the library.  
➢ For our virtual program offerings, there were 16 total registrations across the five 
programs held this month. Some were more popular than others, but it is 
encouraging to see the engagement rising. There are six virtual events planned for 
February, so we will see if the trend continues or starts to dip as the year 
progresses.  
Recurring Program Updates 
➢ Book clubs went well in January. We had steady attendance at both meetings – and 
a few new members joined us!  
o 100 Books Bucket List is discussing The Wind in the Willows by Kenneth 
Grahame at the next meeting on Tuesday March 3rd   
o Between the Bookends is discussing Modoc: The True Story of the Greatest 
Elephant That Ever Lived by Ralph Helfer at the next meeting on Wednesday 
March 11th 
➢ No significant changes to our drop-in tech help sessions with Melanie. There have 
been more patron requests for tech help appointments during other work hours, but 
===PAGE 11===
the workload seems manageable for Melanie for now. We will continue to monitor 
and adjust if needed. 
February Programs 
➢ Adults Only Make It Monthly for February are string art hearts on Wednesday, 
February 4th and Thursday, February 5th. This will be the last of the series before my 
maternity leave, but based on how well this class goes, we may resume when I 
return in the summer. 
➢ The biweekly yoga and breathwork classes continue for February on Thursday, 
February 5th and 19th. 
➢ A popular program, Jason Graham’s Saturday music clinic, will be on February 7th. 
➢ Jill Pottle is returning to teach colored pencil drawing on Sunday, February 8th. 
➢ In conjunction with a teen session earlier in the day, adults can make their own 
copper bookmark with Heather Beck on Thursday, February 12th. 
➢ To celebrate Valentine’s Day, on Friday, February 13th we will host Jeff Rapsis for live 
music accompaniment to silent film “Girl Shy” starring Harold Llyod.  
➢ The Monthly Music Series for February will take place on Wednesday, February 18th 
with an evening of Scottish music. 
➢ Grace Krupkowski with Trilogy Herbalism rescheduled her winter salve making 
workshop from January 30th to February 20th. 
➢ Our annual winter puzzle swap will take place on Saturday, February 21st and we 
already have quite a few collected! 
➢ Following the puzzle swap will be a puzzle race on Wednesday, February 25th. 
➢ We also have several virtual programs running in conjunction with the Ashland 
Library on February 2nd, 7th, 9th, 12th, 23rd, and 26th.

The  IT  department  will  do  a  scheduled  replacement  of  the  two  older  literacy  stations.   ○  Cisco  Umbrella  (filtering)  discussion  Muir  will  be  meeting  with  a  representative  from  Cisco  for  a  demo  of  their  Umbrella  software.  He  has  received  
answers
 
to
 
most
 
of
 
our
 
questions
 
from
 
last
 
month.
 Umbrella  labels  websites  according  to  the  type  of  content  they  contain.  Cisco  Talos  judges  the  content  of  websites  and  assigns  the  labels.  Umbrella  can  be  set  for  a  high,  medium,  or  low  level  of  filtering.  A  white  list  can  be  created,  and  library  staff  can  control  this  list.  We  do  not  have  an  answer  as  to  whether  or  not  we  have  liability  if  we  choose  not  to  censor.  The  Umbrella  software  costs  $2,100/year.  Muir  will  have  more  information  for  us  after  the  demo  and  further  discussion  with  the  Cisco  rep.   ○  Building  &  Maintenance  Update  Work  on  the  card  entry  system  has  started  again.  Passes  will  need  to  be  distributed  to  employees.  The  walls  in  the  Children’s  Room  near  the  chapter  books  have  been  repaired  and  painted.  Jean  Proctor  gave  approval  for  a  bench  in  memory  of  Regina  McGee  to  be  installed  in  the  Proctor  Memorial  
Garden
 
on
 
the
 
library
 
grounds.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
March 19, 2026 
   FY26 Budget Update  
 
   FY27 Budget Update  
This topic is near the top of the list for a number of boards and committees in town. Nothing 
specifically new to the library at the moment, but TriBoard, Selectboard, Fin. Com., and School 
Board are all discussing. 
   Staff Update 
We have performed two rounds of interviews of candidates for our YA Librarian job. With the budget 
uncertainty, the decision is more complicated than usual.  
Joslyn and I both visited a school classroom to read to a class as part of Read Across America. I met 
with Herlin Jaime, Conservation Agetn, to discuss Earth Day partnerships and programs. We plan to 
have a table set up at her Information Fair on April 22, and were requested to purchase a 
performance license for “The Amazing Caterpillar” documentary film, which we will show in May.  
Joslyn and I also met with Kim Sarfde the new Director of Teaching and Learning at the Lunenburg 
Schools to discuss partnerships, especially the 5th grade visits. We are currently running a Minute 
Madness challenge to incentivize reading for the 5th grade. Our current plan is to offer a preliminary 
visit for 5th graders before the end of the school year, then to meet in July with the incoming THES 
principal to further plan for next year and full launch of 5th grade visits. Out of this initial contact, we

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
have also created a “One Community, Many Voices” Reading Event for 8th ELA classes that will be 
help on April 3 at the Library. 
I will be meeting with a videographer from LPACC to discuss a short film featuring the Library.  
We’ve been seeing even higher numbers of teens many days after school. Last Monday and Tuesday 
it was between 30 and 40 kids. A handful of students have also been staying longer than we usually 
see into the evening, sometimes until around 5pm. 
   Programs 
February Vacation turned out to be very successful for out Children’s programs. Winter Painting with 
Julie was full, 21 attended the Afrobeats dance class, and 28 the STEM Olympics. Unfortunately, the 
weather did not cooperate with our rescheduled Winter Salve workshop. 
Adult programs that have been noteworthy include the Puzzle Race, which brought in 17 
participants, the Music Clinic with 15, and the Virtual Women in Jazz talk with 16 attendees, though 
we shared this opportunity to other libraries and many attendees were from other communities. 
Book Groups continue their steady engagement. Our Community Read Brunch was 3/16 and there 
was a nice discussion, though attendance numbers were low. 
Coming up: 3/20 Financial Planning with Edward Jones, 3/21 Final session of Bride W’s Mixed Media 
WKSP, 3/24 Green Screen Movie Making for Teens, 3/26 Minecraft Madnes Show for Kids, 4/6 Tween 
Beadmaking, 4/7 Social Security and Retirement Planning, 4/9 Incredible Worms!, 4/17 Spring 
Tincture, 4/14 Ukulele Meet-Up, 4/14 Teen Paint-your-pot, 4/15 Women of 76 
   Outreach 
Upcoming Events: 4/4 Lunenburg Women’s Club presentation, 4/22 Earth Day Info Booth, 4/27 FSU 
Farmer’s Fair booth, 5/18 Seven Hills Info Fair at Devens, Cruise Night in May, 5/23 Vendor Fair, Artisan 
Market on 5/24, 6/7, 6/21, 7/19, 8/2, 8/30. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
This week the card entry system work has started again. Al has also been working on repairing and 
painting the walls of the Children’s Room near the Chapter Books. 
▪ Technology 
Comcast network upgrading affected the town for two days last week.  
Sherri’s workstation will be replaced with Lydia’s current all-in-one and Lydia will get a new laptop. 
The IT department will also perform scheduled replacement of the older two AWE literacy stations.

Children’s Highlights:  
FEBRUARY BREAK: Winter Painting, Afrobeats Dance Class, and the STEM Olympics were all a 
big hit! Tons of adorable winter bears (and proud faces), dance moves, and gummy bear 
ingenuity :) 
    
 
TODDLER & PRESCHOOL FUN: Toddler Yoga and Kiddie Music Time continue to be highly 
attended and loved. Many returning faces and many new faces for both! I’ve now got a core 
group of kiddos for Around the World Preschool Storytime that come pretty much every week 
(even in the snow!) and are always a riot! Plus, last week’s meeting had a bunch of new faces so 
maybe it will grow even more... 
      
 

===PAGE 9===
Tween/Teen Highlights: 
TWEEN MINECRAFT TOURNAMENT: HUGE success! I had about half of the attendees (or their 
parents) ask me to do it again soon! Despite it being 3 hours, hardly any of the kids were 
restless and probably could have kept playing for a couple more hours... 
 
 
TEEN METALSMITHING AND CUPCAKE DECORATING: A good size group showed up for both 
programs, with only a bit of overlap in attendees. The metalsmithing bookmarks involved a lot 
of concentration and creativity, and they all left with smiles on their faces. It doesn’t take much 
to get teens to like cupcakes (or anyone really), but every single one looked both adorable and 
tasty and sparked some happy munching. We also had a lot of new faces for cupcakes, including 
some older teens!

===PAGE 11===
Trustee Report 
Adult Services Department 
March 2026 
 
Summary of Last Month 
➢ The yoga and breathwork classes really took off in February! We had two full classes 
and lots of positive feedback from participants. 
➢  Our final Adults Only Make It Monthly crafting sessions were also big hits this 
month, drawing a total of 29 people to the library between the two events.  
➢ On February 13th we held a silent film screening of “Girl Shy” with live piano 
accompaniment. There were nine people in attendance, but the program was 
fantastic and something I would highly consider booking here again! Though it 
wasn’t a huge draw, the audience was engaged and the presenter was talented. 
➢ February’s Monthly Music Series concert was on February 18th featuring an evening 
of Scottish music, which was another big hit, and saw 44 people join us. 
➢ We finished the month with two puzzle events: the winter swap on February 21st and 
the puzzle race on February 25th. Both are popular annual programs that drew 55 
and 17 people respectively. Our next puzzling programs are scheduled for May.  
➢ For our virtual program offerings, there were 22 total registrations across the six 
programs held this month. Some were more popular than others, but it is 
encouraging to see the engagement rising. There are eight virtual events planned for 
March, so we will see if the trend continues or starts to dip as the year progresses.  
Recurring Program Updates 
➢ Book clubs went well in February. I informed both groups that March may be the last 
meetings I can attend before maternity leave, and we made plans for how to 
proceed until I return in June/July.   
o 100 Books Bucket List is discussing Alice's Adventures in Wonderland by 
Lewis Carroll at the next meeting on Tuesday April 7th  
o Between the Bookends is discussing The Queens of Crime by Marie Benedict 
at the next meeting on Wednesday April 8th 
➢ No significant changes to our drop-in tech help sessions with Melanie, though her 
tech-help appointments at other times of the week seem to be increasing.  
➢ The Saturday morning music clinics with Jason Graham have also been growing in 
size lately. We routinely see between 15-20 people drop-in to this program that is 
part instruction and part open jam session for players of all skill levels to improve. 
===PAGE 12===
March Programs 
➢ A Lunenburg Cultural Council funded series will be hosted at the library this March: 
Community Mixed Media Art Workshops taught by Bridie Wolejko! There will be 
three sessions: Sunday, March 1st, Friday March 13th, and Saturday March 21st, open 
to artists of all skills to create mixed media artwork as a group. Selected pieces will 
go in an art exhibit for the month of April. Currently, Bridie has chosen some of her 
personal pieces to be displayed in the Wallace Room for March in an exhibit titled: 
“Harmony and Discord: Nature’s Beauty, Humanity’s Impact” 
➢ For the Monthly Music Series this month, we have a virtual concert “Women in Jazz” 
by Flutejuice Productions on the evening of March 5th 
➢ The biweekly yoga and breathwork classes will come to an end this month on 
Thursday, March 5th and 19th 
➢ The annual Community Read partnership with Deb Goodsell at the Lunenburg Adult 
Activity Center continues with a book club discussion and brunch on Monday, 
March 16th. The title selected this year is Food Inc, 2 edited by Karl Weber – a series 
of essays that are companion pieces to the segments of the documentary by the 
same name 
➢ John Hedly will visit on Wednesday, March 18th to give a presentation on beekeeping 
101 
➢ We also have several virtual programs running in conjunction with the Ashland 
Library on March 2nd, 3rd, 9th, 16th, 23rd, 30th, and 31st

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
April 16, 2026 
   FY26 Budget Update  
 
   FY27 Budget Update  
Some sharing of information amongst Library Directors in CWMARS around budget situations. Not 
everyone is in dire straits but many libraries are facing cuts, some will be filing waivers, and many are 
preparing for multiple years of challenging budget context. 
   Staff Update 
Our April 3 partnership with 8th Grade ELA classes was spectacular. Two Trustees in attendance and 
almost 60 people, many more parents than I expected. I’ve already expressed interest in repeating 
the program next year. More than 20 students signed up for library cards as part of the opportunity. 
April 10th Public Access Cable interviewed me and filmed for their next installment of “Welcome 
to…” series. Glad to have the additional effort of spreading word about the library from their team.  
I’ve been considering additional means of studying the unreported/uncaptured data from our 
staffing study last year. I have registered for an online workshop on “Invisible Labor” in hopes that 
there will be some guidance in this context. I’ve also started planning a follow-up supplementary 
study that I would pencil into the schedule for next fall/winter.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
Tomorrow night a staff team will compete in the teen Club’s annual “Are You Smarter than a Sixth 
Grader?” I did also attend a talk from Mass Higher Education Consortium that turned up a new 
vendor for furniture and library materials that I was unaware of, called Mackin that seems promising. 
One topic of discussion at the Friends meeting was the possibility of a donated clothing shed on the 
property. I am awaiting feedback from the Town Manager on whether this would be permitted or 
prohibited. 
I submitted payroll and bills last week, and no obvious errors made. This was the first payroll I’ve 
completed without Lydia since before the pandemic! Scheduling: Marabeth will be out in early June 
with a surgery that she needs. Cheryl should be back from her leave by that point, though, able to 
cover some of those gaps. 
   Programs 
Volunteers from the Friends will help set up and run our May puzzle swap (5/9) with Lydia and Cheryl 
both out on leave during that month. 
We’ve had a number of remarkable programs this past month. Joslyn’s storytimes are really 
knocking it out of the park. Monday of this week had an attendance of 43!  Our April 3 8th grade 
poetry recitation was a big crowd, and created a lot of excitement for poetry. We also showed Come 
See Me in the Good Light, the documentary about poet Andrea Gibson. Small crowd but a great 
format for a poetry event. I have a few more fantastic documentaries about poets that we could 
show in the future. 
The first Ukulele meetup turned out 12 strummers! 31 attendees at the Minecraft Madness show, 45 
came for the drop-in Axolotl Craft, 19 for Incredible Earthworms. The Social Security program was 
attended by a huge number of people, too. At least 75! 
Coming up: 4/17 Spring Tinctures WKSP, Next is our April Vacation programming! 4/21 Spring Painting 
with Julie, 4/22 Seed Kits Take-Home and Terrific Terrariums, 4/22 Arthur James blues guitar, 4/23 
LARPing for kids, 4/24 Video game night. 4/25 Toddler Yoga, 4/28 Uke Meetup, 4/29 Kate Chadbourne 
Poetry & Song, 4/30 Dashbots! 5/1 Sons of Blarney concert, 5/7 What’s Your Next Adventure with 
James Nehring. 
   Outreach 
We broached the subject of merchandise fund raiser sales at the Artisan Market with the Friends. We 
will continue that discussion next month. Seems like an opportunity to raise funds and awareness to 
have people wearing or carrying LPL swag around town. 
Upcoming Events: 4/22 Earth Day Info Booth, 4/27 FSU Farmer’s Fair booth, 5/18 Seven Hills Info Fair 
at Devens, Cruise Night in May, 5/23 Vendor Fair, Artisan Market on 5/24, 6/7, 6/21, 7/19, 8/2, 8/30. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
Entry card system is functioning. We await entry cards that will be printed by the Facilities 
department.  
===PAGE 6===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
The access panels for the water fountains are in the Community Room storage closet, so Wade and I 
moved boxes out of the way for the Facilities team to access. They need to get in there to finish their 
work. It remains to be seen whether the water bubblers will be able to be repaired or if we will need 
to replace both units. Almost all new units are the bottle filler type, though we have a large number 
of children who will not have water bottles, so we might choose to maintain at least one of these 
“old” style bubblers. 
▪ Technology 
Met with Kara and Mason to discuss near future hardware replacement plans for the Library. As we 
mentioned in past meetings, Lydia’s All-in-one will go to Sherri, and Lydia will receive a new laptop. 
Instead of replacing another pair of early literacy stations, I suggested we replace a few of the 
ancient chromeboxes. Although our last Teen Librarian had expressed strong resistance to the idea 
of replacing the devices with new chromeboxes, I see too many reasons that we should stay with 
this format. The three stations in the Teen Room, and three chromebox stations just inside the entry 
to the  Children’s Room are almost always in use during the afternoon and evenings when young 
patrons are visiting the library.

Budget Stats: 
 

===PAGE 8===
 
 
 
Highlights:  
CANDLE MAKING: A fun Saturday of back-to-back candle making! We started off strong with a 
crowd of excited kids making colorful candles (with excellent family feedback)! Then we had a 
small but mighty group of tweens who enjoyed the calmer, more meditative process of melting 
wax and adding spring fragrances. All the kids left happy, and all the tweens came back to pick 
up their settled candles within a couple of days – eager to try them out.  
    
 
DROP-IN CRAFTS: Leprechaun Traps and Paper Roll Axolotls were both a hit. While I haven’t 
heard from any of the kids on whether they successfully trapped a leprechaun (despite me 
specifically requesting updates...), they still left in good spirits (maybe because of the sugar in 
the Lucky Charms but I like to think it was because the craft was so fun). Unsurprisingly, we had 
many eager faces show up to make axolotls: an animal that is currently all the rage with the 
kiddos. Only one person burned themselves with the hot glue gun (me), so it was a success!  
     

===PAGE 9===
 
MINECRAFT MADNESS MAGIC SHOW: Jungle Jim was amazing! He had excellent magic tricks (as 
you can see by the kid’s reactions below), tons of humor, and a natural talent for working with 
kids. I have NEVER seen the kids laugh this much at any program. Even the adults had huge 
smiles and laughs during the grand finale (Jim basically becoming a giant balloon himself). 
   
 
IN-BETWEENERS BOOK CLUB: I had 3 new attendees for April’s book club meeting! Which 
worked out perfectly considering 3 of my regulars had sport conflicts that day... They joined in 
with the rest of the group seamlessly, were visibly disappointed to learn that we only have one 
meeting left this year, and demanded (along with the others) that we bring the book club back 
in the fall... So, I already have some September plans it seems!

 ○  Technology  Update    Lydia’s  new  laptop  has  arrived.  Sherri  is  now  using  Lydia’s  old  workstation.   ■  Cisco  Umbrella  Discussion  Muir  is  still  conducting  a  trial  of  the  software.  There  have  been  a  handful  of  websites  blocked  for  
adult
 
content.
 The  trial  is  not  network  wide.  You  cannot  tell  which  computer  is  trying  to  access  a  website  that  is  blocked.  The  software  can  be  set  to  filter  at  the  device  level  or  the  network  level.  Device  level  filtering  would  
affect
 
fewer
 
users.
 
Network
 
level
 
filtering
 
would
 
filter
 
any
 
device
 
connected
 
to
 
our
 
network.
 
People
 
can
 
still
 
use
 
their
 
own
 
data
 
connection
 
on
 
their
 
personal
 
device
 
to
 
avoid
 
the
 
filter.
 Muir  is  not  certain  if  malicious  use  of  AI  would  be  blocked.  Stephanie  is  concerned  that  allowing  even  a  low  level  of  filtering  could  eventually  lead  to  increased  
censorship
 
as
 
our
 
political
 
climate
 
changes.

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
May 21, 2026 
   FY26 Budget Update  
 
   FY27 Budget Update  
FY27 Library Budget will be $598,960.20. No overrides passed on the ballot and we will operate on 
the balanced budget that was passed at Annual Town Meeting. This is approximately a 5% decrease 
on FY26. We will not be able to hire a Young Adult Librarian. 
   Staff Update 
This week will be the return of 5th grade visits on Tuesday and Thursday. This will be the first time 
since 2020 that we’ve been able to make these visits happen. We will meet with the new THES 
administration this summer to prepare and plan for a more comprehensive set of visits next school 
year. 
Recent weeks have certainly focused on the FY27 budget and preparations for Annual Town 
Meeting, which happened May 2. This year was an approximately 8 hour meeting. The Selectboard 
offered an informal info session on the Tuesday before. This novel format did bring out some visitors, 
perhaps three dozen. All who I spoke to were staunch Library supporters. 
Main Street Bank has moved to an every other year award structure for their community support 
grants, and they chose to not fund our summer Lunch Program this year. The Friends did vote to 
provide the funds. I also met with an Assistant Vice President from Worker’s Credit Union to discuss

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
potential corporate sponsorships. I focused that conversation on the lunch program, but also 
included a number of other projects.  
I met with the new Rec Director and Deb at COA to talk about coordinating programs. Discussed the 
possibility of partnering on summer concerts that could be held outdoors in conjunction with her 
Monday evening dinners, if the Town band concerts do not occur. 
Staff and I will work on a plan to cover the Teen Room in a more complete way for next school year.  
   Programs 
Joslyn and I have found volunteers to help us offer two craft workshop series over the summer for 
Teens. We will have a 3d printing series and a crochet series led by volunteers. Similarly, our Friends 
volunteers helped us make our Spring puzzle swap successful. Over 800 puzzles and almost 60 
attendees this time. 
Poetry month came to a lovely conclusion with Kate Chadbourne’s performance. We had success 
with Earth Day events, too: 19 for Incredible Earthworms, 24 for Terrific Terrariums, and 31 for our 
Mother’s Day Planting. All of our Three Sisters Seed Kits (about 80) were given out, too! The Social 
Security program was attended by a huge number of people, too. At least 75! LARPing for kids also 
had 24 attendees. 
Our recent Teen programs had solid attendance as well. 20 came to the Paint-a-pot event, and Video 
game night had 14 attendees. 
Our monthly concert series continues to be one of our larger adult events. 22 and 35 at the last two 
concerts.  
Coming up: 4/17 Spring Tinctures WKSP, Next is our April Vacation programming! 4/21 Spring Painting 
with Julie, 4/22 Seed Kits Take-Home and Terrific Terrariums, 4/22 Arthur James blues guitar, 4/23 
LARPing for kids, 4/24 Video game night. 4/25 Toddler Yoga, 4/28 Uke Meetup, 4/29 Kate Chadbourne 
Poetry & Song, 4/30 Dashbots! 5/1 Sons of Blarney concert, 5/7 What’s Your Next Adventure with 
James Nehring. 
   Outreach 
The Earth Day Info Fair was our high point for outreach this month, Herlin Jaime organized a 
fabulous event with some vendors traveling quite a distance, and the gym was very active with 
visitors.  
April 27 FSU Farmer’s Market and Info Fair and May 18 Seven Hills Transition to Adult Hood Info Fair. 
Both enabled us to gather some informal data on needs in our community. 
Upcoming Events: Cruise Night May 20, 5/30 Vendor Fair, Artisan Market on 5/24, 6/7, 6/21, 7/19, 8/2, 
8/30. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
===PAGE 9===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
New water bubblers and bottle filler are a huge hit. Many patrons have given us positive feedback 
and are excited about these new additions. Two unfortunate events this month, though. The Bob 
Cole giraffe sculpture was damaged. No one was hurt, but two young boys did get a bit scared as it 
fell because of their pulling on it. I anticipate that we will be able to repair it successfully, and have 
several potential sources of help. Second, six of our marsh marigold plants were taken from the 
rainwater garden in the rear of the library, next to the TCP driveway. I made a Facebook post 
requesting their return and will follow up with Chief Thibodeau on Friday, if no other developments 
occur.  
I am planning to purchase furniture to refresh the Story Craft Room with funds from State Aid to 
Libraries.  
▪ Technology 
Lydia’s new laptop awaits her return, and Sherri has had zero issues with Lydia’s old workstation. 
These were setup last week. IT will order a monitor with integrated docking station to facilitate her 
work processes.

Patron Quotes:  
“My daughter demanded that we stop at the library every day this week. I think it’s her new 
safe space.” 
 
Budget Stats: 
 

===PAGE 11===
 
 
 
Child Highlights:  
APRIL BREAK: Julie Harrold returned on Tuesday to, once again, instruct a class full of focused 
faces and impressive pieces of art! Julie’s classes are always followed by demands for more. On 
Wednesday we have NEBG at Tower Hill for a filled-up terrarium program. I was surprised by 
just how cute every single terrarium turned out and heard only good things from kids and 
parents. On Thursday The Story School hosted a King Arthur inspired LARP adventure. Chris of 
The Story School is great with the kids and was able to force the kids to use their minds to solve 
puzzles as well as get out all their energy on the villainous Red Dragon with their foam swords. 
The kids were so enthused that a large group even stayed behind insisting on taking a group 
photo (featured below). Plus, I got to play a role in the story and was having just as much as all 
the kids. Overall, it was a very lively and happy April Break week!  
   
   

===PAGE 12===
 
DASHBOTS: “Robot Soccer” was a big hit (a surprise to no one). There was shockingly good 
teamwork and tons of laughs as they attempted to program their robots to score goals.  
    
 
CATERPILLARS: While it’s quite possible that the person who enjoyed the caterpillars the most 
was me... I promise the kids enjoyed it too! While I was hoping they would last just a little 
longer so I could release them on our upcoming Caterpillar Day (that I believe at the time of this 
meeting has already happened...), they managed to grow and transform quite quickly. One 
child who visited the library two days in a row almost didn’t believe me when I insisted they 
were the same caterpillars from the day before. Luckily, I was able to release them at my final 
Sounds and Music storytime for the year. The toddlers were enthralled through all 10 releases!  
   
 
TWEEN/TEEN Highlights: 
TWEEN GAME NIGHT (AGAIN!): Another success! Opening this event up to the tweens has 
proved to be a worthwhile change. Good attendance, happy faces, and plenty of new folks! 
  
 

===PAGE 13===
TEEN PAINT-YOUR-OWN-POT: Many after-school teens were pleasantly surprised by stumbling 
across this program. It didn't take much to convince them to participate! Thank you to Lydia P 
for the idea :)

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
June 18, 2026 
   FY26 Budget Update  
 
   FY27 Budget Update  
FY2027 is open for purchase requisitions, and we have been introduced to an improved process for 
Accounts Payable and invoice processing.  
   Staff Update 
The Town offered a full-day training on June 9, with sections on CivicsPlus (Town website), Suzor IT 
Help, and MUNIS. I attended and will share the CivicsPlus details with Melanie, though we do not use 
the Town’s website for much. 
User’s Council occurred on June 2, with a handful of votes, including a renewal of by-laws. News of 
Clinton’s voting down their Library’s building project clouded the event. Moods were slightly buoyed 
with reports from MBLC and MLS about advocacy efforts. 
Marabeth and Cheryl have both returned to duty. Lydia and I discussed her return at the end of the 
month. Thanks again to Debbie for sticking around in her retirement and continuing to help us out.  
North End Subaru has committed to another year of support for the Monthly Music Series and 
increased their funding level to $400 per event. They have also approved use of their funds for an 
outside, off-site series during the summer, if I can work with Deb at COA and make that happen on

LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
short notice. I plan to meet with the Town Manager on a more regular basis to improve 
communication, with meetings every other week penciled in to the schedule. 
   Programs 
Summer Reading registration has launched with quite a lot of interest. We registered nearly 200 
children by the end of the second week that registrations have been open. Teen numbers have been 
slow to increase, perhaps related to the conclusion of the school year. We have a couple dozen 
adults already registered, too. A number of residents registering for new cards have also expressed 
interest in Summer Reading. We have added second sessions for nearly all summer events for 
children, too, as regisrations filled up in the first two days for almost everything. We will use State 
Aid funding to cover these extra sessions. 
Hello Summer Henna was again popular, drawing 48 attendees. Although a number of registrants 
did not show up, Joslyn’s Summer Book Bag Decoration program had 72 attendees! 32 attended 
Wacky Weather with Kosmic Kelly and 13 came to Caterpillar Day and our screening of Amazing 
Caterpillar, even though we had to release the butterflies we hatched early. For teens, one scheduled 
of the Yoga drew a dozen participants, but then the second was scheduled to close to the end of the 
year. There does seem to be interested so far in our Crochet and 3D printing clubs during SRP, too! 
We had a full workshop to make felted sheep on June 10th, and Stitchcraft and Book clubs have been 
steady for adults. Concerts have brought in solid numbers the last few months. North End Subaru 
committed to another year of funding at $400 per event (an increase of $100).  
Coming up: SRP full launch 6/22! 6/18 Monthly Music: Pop Rock of the 60s and 70s, 6/19 closed for 
Juneteenth. Teens: 3d printing club on Mondays, Crochet Tuesday afternoons. Children: Make-It 
Mondays, Lego Tuesdays, Therapy Dog Wednesdays, Big Event Thursdays. 6/25 SRP Kickoff: Ice 
Cream Social (after free lunch) 7/9 Animal Adventures (with a wallaby!). 7/15 Monthly Music Series: 
Irish Folk Music, 7/27 Make It Monday: Unicorn Snot, 7/30 LED Mini Golf and Games (Finale). 
   Outreach 
The Earth Day Info Fair was our high point for outreach this month, Herlin Jaime organized a 
fabulous event with some vendors traveling quite a distance, and the gym was very active with 
visitors.  
April 27 FSU Farmer’s Market and Info Fair and May 18 Seven Hills Transition to Adult Hood Info Fair. 
Both enabled us to gather some informal data on needs in our community. 
Upcoming Events: Artisan Market on 6/21, 7/19, 8/2, 8/30. Town Beach TBD. 
▪ Building & Maintenance 
A number of Facilities projects have seen delays this spring. Our water fountain is still awaiting 
service. The bathroom stalls that were ordered came in the wrong color. Don has been trying to find 
a replacement for the baby changing station in the Men’s Room, but sizing has been incorrect and 
the original is no longer made. Al is out on leave and will continue painting projects around the 
Library when he returns. 
===PAGE 7===
LUNENBURG PUBLIC LIBRARY DIRECTOR’S REPORT 
We received an anonymous gift to replace the marsh marigolds that went missing in May. What a 
generous and kind person to do this! 
▪ Technology 
Cisco Umbrella licensing has been purchased. Our trial deployment remains in effect as CWMARS will 
be installing a new router this summer that will allow us better filtering management. 
We will be purchasing a poster printer that can print up to 24” in-house. This service will be available 
to staff, patrons and other departments. We will be capable of matte or gloss paper printing, and 
vinyl (banners). Schedule of fees TBD.

Highlights:  
MINDFUL YOGA FOR TEENS: We had such an unexpected number of teens show up that the 
instructor had to make a trip back to her car to get more yoga mats! There was a good mix of 
kids (gender, age, newbies vs frequenters, etc.) with the newbies giving rave reviews 
afterwards! Bonus: Kat, the instructor, was proud of some of the boys who have shown interest 
but had a couple of behavioral issues during past sessions. A great example of how consistency 
can build both interest and trust with the teens! 
 
SUMMER READING OFF TO A GREAT START: Minna and I had a fantastic visit to the Primary 
School! The kids were so full of energy that we had to hold for applause after everything we 
said! And it seems to have worked... We have had an overwhelming response to our summer 
reading events! We’ve even had to add second sessions to a few programs. I can’t blame 
everyone for wanting to do a craft with me, but we’re going to need a lot of glue for our 
Unicorn Snot slime... We are also up to 207 SRP registrations! 
 
 
WACKY WEATHER W/ KOSMIC KELLY: Kosmic Kelly is karismatic, kreative, and klever! The kids 
were thoroughly entertained by her weather show and, thankfully, very interested in both the 
spectacle and the science behind it. Hopefully it was a room full of future scientists!  
   
 
LAST TODDLER YOGA: We had some frequenters and some surprised patrons who just 
happened to show up on a Saturday and then we roped into doing yoga... But everyone was 
delighted! Every session has received very positive feedback with 2MindfulGirls’ being fantastic 

===PAGE 10===
with the kiddos – but this one received the most praise (along with demands to have them back 
next school year). Might have to be a recurring event! 
 
 
HENNA: As always, Mandy’s henna was quite popular and loved! She always brings tons of 
designs and works at an unbelievably impressive speed allowing her to do henna for around 50 
people in just a 2-hour time span. Even the toddlers who got henna didn’t have the time to get 
impatient. 
    
  
DIY SUMMER READING BOOK BAGS: I could not have predicted this event’s popularity... I even 
had to go and buy double the number of bags! And it was worth it for all the highly enthused 
young artists ready to take home some summer reading books in their brand-new bags
